commit d14d778446b5cbc4496cb9a3429009ca0f1134b8
Author: SounMayon <slimienicay@gmail.com>
Date:   Mon Nov 23 10:55:16 2026 +0100

    Synthesize time based effects for the OS sink; optional FFB rate diagnostics
    
    haptic_core_update(): for sinks flagged E_HAPTIC_SINK_CAP_SYNTHESIS (the OS/SDL sink) the waveforms
    and the constant forces of the 4 slots are summed into one constant force, gain and invert applied;
    effects that end by themselves are reported back to the source. A physical G29 still gets the native
    report. With HAPTIC_STATS (make HAPTIC_STATS=1), the path is instrumented: core update, synth step,
    sink process/update and the SDL call (duration, return value). Without it, haptic_stats.c is not built.

 core/Makefile                     |  9 ++++++
 core/haptic/haptic_core.c         | 58 +++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++
 core/haptic/sink/haptic_sink_os.c | 17 ++++++++++--
 3 files changed, 82 insertions(+), 2 deletions(-)

commit ed96fe02fe63e933c218626b1505e7fdc7192734
Author: SounMayon <slimienicay@gmail.com>
Date:   Mon Nov 23 10:54:31 2026 +0100

    Decode the time based Logitech forces; auto-center and friction conversion; source ended() hook
    
    ff_lg_convert_force(): sawtooth, trapezoid, rectangle, square, ramp and variable (slots 0 and 2) carry
    their decoded parameters in the core data (the type stays LOGITECH_RAW so that a G29 sink forwards the
    native report). Auto-center spring is converted to a spring and friction to a damper (approximations).
    haptic_source_lg: ended() resets the playing state of a slot whose effect ended by itself, so that an
    identical DOWNLOAD_AND_PLAY or a PLAY restarts it.

 core/haptic/common/ff_lg.c            | 109 ++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++
 core/haptic/source/haptic_source_lg.c |  12 +++-
 2 files changed, 120 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-)

commit 8f467548381f99133999372ad0523c1c1151341c
Author: SounMayon <slimienicay@gmail.com>
Date:   Mon Nov 23 10:52:07 2026 +0100

    Add time based Logitech effects evaluator, synthesizer and FFB diagnostics modules
    
    New modules, not called yet: haptic_waveform (sawtooth, trapezoid, rectangle, square, ramp, variable),
    haptic_synth (sums constants and waveforms of the 4 slots into one constant force), haptic_stats
    (rate diagnostics, built only with HAPTIC_STATS). Headers: decoded waveform in s_haptic_core_data,
    E_HAPTIC_SINK_CAP_SYNTHESIS, optional ended() hook of the sources.

=== ROADMAP.md ===
# Roadmap: GIMX Reborn, PC and PS4 G29 force feedback

Last updated: 2026-11-22

## Scope and current branches

- Application: [SounMayon/GIMX, `GIMX-Reborn`](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/tree/GIMX-Reborn)
- Firmware: [SounMayon/GIMX-firmwares, `G29-Finished`](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX-firmwares/tree/G29-Finished/EMUG29PS4)
- Documentation: [GIMX wiki](https://gimx.fr/wiki/index.php?title=Main_Page)

## Final acceptance goal: G29 FFB on PC and PS4

The project must support PC and PS4 with effects reaching a physical wheel connected through GIMX. On PC, FFB test software must discover every target effect, create/configure it, start and stop it, and produce the expected force. On PS4, retain G29 enumeration/authentication and pass through every FFB command sent by supported games so its expected force reaches the physical wheel. Merely advertising effects or preserving enumeration does not count as FFB support.

Use the USB HID PID 1.0 effect set as the coverage target: constant, ramp, custom force, square, sine, triangle, sawtooth up/down, spring, damper, inertia and friction. Microsoft's GameInput HID force-feedback path aligns with PID 1.0. Logitech documents the real G29's PC-mode identity as VID/PID 046D:C24F. Before implementation, verify whether Windows test software accepts PID reports with that identity or requires Logitech's native report protocol. Test PC discovery and commands with Windows DirectInput/GameInput tools. Test PS4 compatibility separately with supported games and USB/serial captures; the PC effect list is not assumed to be generated by PS4 games.

## Progress

### Done

- Added Ubuntu 26.04 GitHub Actions builds for the application and the `EMUG29PS4` AVR firmware.
- Added the full recursive submodule checkout to the application build workflow.
- Confirmed the PS4 firmware already forwards interrupt OUT reports through the adapter serial protocol (`BYTE_OUT_REPORT`); the application can consume the game's native Logitech commands without changing firmware framing.
- Added current Linux build instructions in [BUILDING.md](BUILDING.md).
- Baseline application build passed on Ubuntu 26.04 in [GitHub Actions run 43323280446](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/actions/runs/43323280446) at commit `d5c9407`.
- Application build also passes with compiler/library version reporting enabled in [run 43324543826](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/actions/runs/43324543826).
- Baseline `EMUG29PS4` firmware build passed on GitHub Actions in [run 43320703237](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX-firmwares/actions/runs/43320703237) using the existing source and vendored LUFA.
- After that baseline, `EMUG29PS4` was moved to upstream LUFA `LUFA-170418`; the updated firmware and AVR toolchain version report pass in [run 43324588408](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX-firmwares/actions/runs/43324588408).
- Added Linux build instructions that include the maintained patches for the pinned HID and USB submodules.
- Registered physical G29 PC (`046d:C24F`) and PS4 (`046d:C260`) IDs with the Logitech FFB output sink so decoded constant, spring and damper effects use its native reports; build passed in [run 43337088198](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/actions/runs/43337088198).
- Added a native-report path for timed variable and other Logitech force types the generic model cannot represent. It preserves the four source effect slots and forwards these reports to a matching physical G29; Linux build passed in [run 43337925390](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/actions/runs/43337925390), and the grouped-stop follow-up passed in [run 43338022752](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/actions/runs/43338022752).
- Hardened the PS4 Logitech source path by rejecting truncated output reports before parsing and correcting FIFO removal to shift using `FIFO_SIZE` and clear the actual tail slot; Linux and Windows builds pass in [run 43342959350](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/actions/runs/43342959350) and [run 43342959337](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/actions/runs/43342959337).

- Implemented the missing Logitech classic effects in the application haptic engine (branch `gimx-reborn-ffb`, not yet built in CI nor tested on hardware):
  - `core/haptic/haptic_waveform.{c,h}`: pure, clock-independent evaluator for sawtooth up/down, trapezoid, rectangle, square, ramp and variable forces, computed from the elapsed time (Logitech main loop assumed to be 2 ms).
  - `core/haptic/haptic_synth.{c,h}` and `haptic_core_update()`: sinks flagged `E_HAPTIC_SINK_CAP_SYNTHESIS` (the generic OS/SDL sink) get these effects synthesized as one constant force, summed with the constant forces of the four slots and with gain/invert tweaks applied. Other sinks are unchanged, and a physical G29 still receives the native report.
  - `ff_lg_convert_force()`: auto-center spring (0x03) is converted to a spring and friction (0x0e) to a damper (approximations); waveform types stay `E_DATA_TYPE_LOGITECH_RAW` and carry the decoded parameters.
  - Unit tests: `make -C core/test/haptic test` (analytic expectations, no hardware needed; the current coverage is described in the review follow-up below).
  - Hypotheses to confirm on hardware or with captures: rectangle phase P semantics, square wave starting in the high state, sawtooth wrap-around span, ramp stopping at the target. Not covered: inertia (not part of the Logitech classic protocol), envelopes, effect direction, and a velocity-based friction model.

- Review follow-up on the time based effects (`gimx-reborn-ffb`): a slot whose effect ended by itself (ramp target reached, finite square wave) is now reported back to the Logitech source (`s_haptic_source.ended`), so an identical `DOWNLOAD_AND_PLAY` or a `PLAY` restarts it instead of being ignored as "already playing". Tests now cover this lifecycle with the real Logitech source, the first wrap-around values of both sawtooth waves, the rectangle phase (offset and out of range), variable forces 0 and 2 (and the unsupported slots 1 and 3), several simultaneous waveforms with different start times, and positive/negative saturation (71 assertions). The tests check internal consistency and the documented hypotheses, not conformity to a real G29.
- Restitution rate of synthesized effects: the waveform clock is the Logitech main loop (2 ms), but the force is only recomputed when `haptic_core_update()` runs. It runs after each received FFB report and once per controller refresh period, which is 5 ms by default for the PS4 G29 (minimum 1000 µs, see `shared/gimxcontroller/src/g29_ps4.c`). Waveforms with a period shorter than twice the refresh period are therefore aliased, and a fast square wave can be missed. Next step: measure the real send rate to the SDL sink and decide whether to average the waveform over the elapsed interval or to require a smaller refresh period.

- Rate diagnostics (`gimx-reborn-ffb`): `core/haptic/haptic_stats.{c,h}` and `make HAPTIC_STATS=1` instrument the whole path (core update, synth step/result, `sink->process`, `sink->update`, `ginput_joystick_set_haptic()` with its duration and return value) with histograms (power of two buckets above 51.2 ms) and rings of requested / attempted / accepted transitions, nothing logged per event. Without `HAPTIC_STATS` the module is not built and the core has no diagnostic field. The OS sink `update()` is a no-op and all SDL work is synchronous inside `process()`; the return value of `ginput_joystick_set_haptic()` used to be ignored. Protocol and report template: `doc/FFB_rate_measurement.md`. `core/test/haptic/haptic_rate_sim` is a computation level prediction only (real synthesizer, simulated ticks, mock sink): at a 5 ms tick a square wave keeps 100 % of its transitions from a 6 ms half period, about 60 % at 4 ms and about 20 % at 2 ms; at 2 ms and 1 ms ticks all are delivered. Real SDL/OS rates still have to be measured on hardware before changing the synthesizer rate.

### In progress

- Finish inventorying other bundled/shared dependencies and decide whether they need independent upgrades; the current G29-only LUFA update does not change other firmware targets.
- PC target output is confirmed: effects must reach a physical wheel connected through GIMX. Standard USB HID PID 1.0 is the first candidate for broad PC effect discovery. The real G29 is identified as 046D:C24F in PC mode, but compatibility between that identity, PID reports and Windows test software must be verified before changing firmware behavior.
- PS4 remains a required target: preserve G29 enumeration/authentication. Current native pass-through handles source effect reports on a physical G29; the PC/CI builds cannot prove the PS4 device path or physical force. Validate it with USB/serial captures and real hardware.
- Upstream Linux recognizes a G29 mode, but its hid-lg4ff driver exposes only FF_CONSTANT and autocenter to Linux's input force-feedback API. This describes that driver's supported interface; it does not establish the wheel hardware's full capability or Windows behavior. Use it as a reason to test the Windows protocol directly, not as a hardware capability limit. See [upstream hid-lg4ff.c](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/master/drivers/hid/hid-lg4ff.c).
- Validate G29 behavior on hardware after the compiled LUFA update; the GitHub build cannot validate enumeration, authentication or force feedback.
- Added a Windows UCRT64 GitHub Actions build using current MSYS2 packages; it now passes in [run 43342959337](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/actions/runs/43342959337). Bringing the legacy code up to this toolchain required downstream portability fixes for GCC 16 pointer/time formats, SDL2's `SDL_HAPTIC_STEERING_AXIS`, and wxWidgets 3.2 static command-line descriptors, plus an explicit downloader integer type. The run used GCC/G++ 16.2.0, wxWidgets 3.2.11, SDL2 2.32.10, libxml2 2.15.4, libusb 1.0.30, libcurl 8.23.0, ncurses 6.6 and gettext 1.0. MSYS2 is rolling, so CI records exact package versions on every run. See [SDL2 steering axis](https://wiki.libsdl.org/SDL2/SDL_HAPTIC_STEERING_AXIS).
- Keep protocol and firmware behavior changes separate from dependency updates so each step can be compiled and reviewed.

## Audit notes

### Application and dependencies

The project is a C/C++ Makefile build with shared Git submodules. System dependencies include wxWidgets, SDL2, libxml2, libcurl, BlueZ, ncurses, mhash, libusb, X11/Xi and gettext. The Ubuntu 26.04 workflow resolves package versions from the current Ubuntu repositories.

The successful baseline exposed and fixed these issues under the current compiler and libraries:
- The pinned `gimxhid` and `gimxusb` submodules freed libusb poll descriptors before rollback accessed them. The fixes are maintained in this repository under `patches/` and applied by CI and the documented Linux build.
- The root Makefile did not make `build-loader` depend on `build-shared`, allowing parallel linking before `gimxtime` existed.
- Linux depended on the removed `ncursesw5-config` executable. The core Makefile now supports ncursesw5, ncursesw6 and pkg-config.
- Current compiler diagnostics exposed missing standard-library declarations and mismatched/no-prototype Bluetooth APIs; the affected includes, declarations and call sites are corrected.

The Ubuntu 26.04 Linux application build is green. The version-reporting step recorded GCC/G++ 15.2.0, wxWidgets 3.2.9, SDL2 2.32.10, libxml2 2.15.2, libusb 1.0.29, ncursesw 6.6, libcurl 8.18.0 and BlueZ 5.85. Current UCRT64 build passes on [run 43342959337](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/actions/runs/43342959337) with GCC 16.2.0, wxWidgets 3.2.11, SDL2 2.32.10, libxml2 2.15.4, libusb 1.0.30, libcurl 8.23.0 and ncurses 6.6. It exercises the downstream portability fixes for the pinned Windows priority/time/input submodules and the three wxWidgets command-line descriptors. The Linux build for the same code is green on [run 43342959350](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/actions/runs/43342959350).

The AVR workflow recorded avr-gcc 14.3.0, avr-libc 2.2.1, binutils-avr 2.43.50 and make 4.4.1.

### PC USB PID gap

- [Descriptors.c](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX-firmwares/blob/G29-Finished/EMUG29PS4/Descriptors.c) advertises Generic Desktop, Button and Logitech vendor pages. The report descriptor has no PID Usage Page `0x0F` or standard Set Effect/Periodic/Condition report collections, so it does not currently advertise standard HID PID effects to a PC.
- Microsoft documents GameInput force feedback over HID as aligned with USB PID 1.0. Logitech's G29 product specification identifies `046D:C24F` as PC mode. These facts support a standard PID implementation as the first design candidate, but do not prove that Windows will route every test application through PID when using the Logitech identity. Verify enumeration and effect creation on Windows before settling descriptor identity and protocol. References: [Microsoft GameInput FFB](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/gaming/gdk/docs/features/common/input/hardware/input-hardware-force-feedback?view=gdk-2604), [Logitech G29 specifications](https://support.logi.com/hc/en-us/articles/360023463753-G29-Driving-Force-Racing-Wheel-Technical-Specifications).
- [emu.c](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX-firmwares/blob/G29-Finished/EMUG29PS4/emu.c) handles the existing vendor feature IDs `F0-F3`; it does not allocate PID effect blocks or process standard PID output reports.
- The application haptic core currently models rumble, constant, spring and damper (plus LEDs/range). The Logitech conversion supports only constant, a limited variable-to-constant case, spring, damper and high-resolution spring/damper. Ramp, periodic waveforms, custom force, inertia and friction are not represented in the generic core.
- The generic application model and OS/SDL sink still expose only rumble, constant, spring and damper; there is no general periodic scheduler or motion-aware inertia/friction model. For the PS4 G29 source, unmodeled Logitech reports now bypass that model only when the output is a matching physical G29. Other physical wheels still require explicit effect conversion or synthesis.
- PC support therefore requires coordinated firmware descriptor/report handling, an expanded effect model, parameter-block lifecycle and effect execution, plus a defined route to the physical wheel. Keep the PS4 vendor protocol as a separate regression path and compile both targets after each step.
- Protocol decision checkpoint: test standard HID PID enumeration and all target effect creation on Windows using the intended PC identity. If the Logitech identity selects a native-only path, compare a generic PID identity and the real G29 descriptor/report capture before choosing a compatible architecture.
- USB-IF references: [PID Class Definition 1.0](https://www.usb.org/sites/default/files/documents/pid1_01.pdf) and [HID Usage Tables](https://www.usb.org/sites/default/files/hut1_3_0.pdf).

### Odrive-Wheel as the DIY physical FFB output

- The [Odrive-Wheel `feature/t300-hef4021` firmware](https://github.com/SounMayon/Odrive-Wheel/tree/feature/t300-hef4021) uses USB VID/PID `1209:0D40` and exposes a composite HID FFB plus CDC device. Its USB identity is not the Logitech G29 identity used by the PS4 emulator.
- Its HID FFB implementation handles effect creation, constant, ramp, periodic and condition reports, and its effects calculator renders periodic waveforms and position/velocity/acceleration-based forces. Custom-force and sample-download reports currently have no implementation, so complete HID PID coverage is not established.
- GIMX's generic OS sink advertises only rumble, constant, spring and damper. It warns and drops `LOGITECH_RAW`; the Logitech sink forwards native reports only for Logitech G29 output IDs. Therefore the current GIMX branch cannot deliver every PS4-native G29 report to this Odrive device. Some modeled effects may work through the OS haptic API, but that path has not been verified with this firmware.
- Required integration: add and validate a dedicated `1209:0D40` output path that converts the G29 source reports into effects the Odrive HID PID endpoint accepts, including update/play/stop, timing, gain and effect-slot lifecycle. Preserve direct G29 pass-through as a separate path.
- On a PC, the Odrive firmware presents its own HID PID identity; actual FFB use depends on host/game support. Flashing `EMUG29PS4.hex` onto the separate GIMX adapter does not change the Odrive USB identity. The PS4 emulator descriptor and Odrive output device are different USB devices and roles.

### Existing G29 PS4 emulation

- The firmware descriptor advertises Logitech `046d:C260` and interrupt input/output endpoints in [Descriptors.c](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX-firmwares/blob/G29-Finished/EMUG29PS4/Descriptors.c#L95-L105).
- The firmware handles HID feature requests `F1/F2` in [emu.c](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX-firmwares/blob/G29-Finished/EMUG29PS4/emu.c#L32-L45), sends a serial header and spins until `spoofReply` arrives. There is no timeout or recovery path, so a lost reply can stall that request.
- The application already registers a G29 PS4 controller, builds wheel/pedal/button reports, handles authentication and sends reports at a 5 ms default interval.
- FFB is not universally flattened to constant: the Logitech parser maps `CONSTANT`, `SPRING` and `DAMPER` to distinct core types; simple `VARIABLE` forces are approximated as constant. Timed `VARIABLE` and other Logitech force types now retain their original report for native pass-through to a physical G29. For other wheel models, unknown reports still are not translated. See [the conversions](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/blob/GIMX-Reborn/core/haptic/common/ff_lg.c#L385-L497) and [Logitech report encoding](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/blob/GIMX-Reborn/core/haptic/common/ff_lg.c#L550-L655).
- GIMX's guide documents working wheel, pedal, wheel-button and shifter controls, combined input devices, and force feedback; the GIMX path is an existing G29 emulator that needs validation and robustness work. See the [G29 to PS4 guide](https://gimx.fr/wiki/index.php?connectiontype=USB&device=SteeringWheel&ostype=windows&platform=ps4&title=Guide) and [force feedback notes](https://gimx.fr/wiki/index.php?title=Force_Feedback).
- Highest-value additions: bound control/serial lengths, add a timeout and recoverable error path for `F1/F2`, test report boundaries and reconnect behavior, compare descriptors/reports with a genuine G29 capture, then validate authentication and force feedback on a real G29, adapter and PS4. Do not change framing or timing without captures and hardware validation.
- Firmware baseline used bundled LUFA `130901`. After both baseline builds passed, only the G29 target was switched to the pinned upstream `LUFA-170418` submodule. Its build passes with the descriptor callback updated to LUFA's `uint16_t wIndex` signature; other firmware targets still use the existing shared LUFA tree.

## Next steps

1. Preserve the green Linux, Windows/UCRT64 and AVR baselines; keep the current CI results as the compile checkpoint before protocol or firmware changes.
2. Resolve PC protocol compatibility on Windows: test the target PC identity and the real G29's descriptor/report behavior, then choose standard HID PID, native Logitech reports or a measured combination. Record the test application and observable capabilities.
3. Specify the selected PC descriptor/report IDs, effect pool strategy, capabilities and route to physical output against USB-IF PID 1.0 and the GIMX haptic sink.
4. Extend the shared haptic model and firmware/application transport for every PC target effect; implement effect allocation, parameter updates, gain/envelopes, operation/status and motion-aware condition effects.
5. Validate PS4 FFB delivery with reports captured from supported games and a physical G29; extend translation for non-G29 physical wheel sinks so timed and periodic effects are rendered where supported.
6. Build a Windows test matrix that enumerates each PC target effect, creates/configures it, starts/stops it and checks the resulting force. Retain the PS4 firmware/application paths as regression checks.
7. Validate PC and PS4 descriptors/reports with USB captures, then verify PC target effects and supported PS4 game effects on the physical wheel.
8. Update this roadmap after every implementation step and CI result.

=== WORKFLOWS ===
==> .github/workflows/build-linux.yml <==
name: Linux build

on:
  push:
  pull_request:
  workflow_dispatch:

permissions:
  contents: read

jobs:
  build:
    runs-on: ubuntu-26.04
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
        with:
          submodules: recursive
      - name: Install current Ubuntu development packages
        run: |
          sudo apt-get update
          sudo apt-get install --yes \
            build-essential pkg-config git gettext \
            libwxgtk3.2-dev libsdl2-dev libxml2-dev libusb-1.0-0-dev \
            libbluetooth-dev libncurses-dev libmhash-dev \
            libcurl4-openssl-dev libxi-dev libx11-dev xterm xdg-utils
      - name: Record toolchain and library versions
        run: |
          gcc --version | head -n1
          g++ --version | head -n1
          wx-config --version
          for package in sdl2 libxml-2.0 libusb-1.0 ncursesw libcurl; do
            printf '%s ' "$package"
            pkg-config --modversion "$package"
          done
          dpkg-query -W -f='${binary:Package} ${Version}\n' libbluetooth-dev libmhash-dev libxi-dev libx11-dev
      - name: Build
        run: make -j2

+# Building GIMX on Ubuntu 26.04
+
+This branch uses the current package versions from the Ubuntu 26.04 repositories. The GitHub Actions workflow installs the build dependencies and compiles the complete project, including its shared Git submodules.
+
+The same steps can be run manually:
+
+```sh
+sudo apt-get update
+sudo apt-get install --yes \
+  build-essential pkg-config git gettext \
+  libwxgtk3.2-dev libsdl2-dev libxml2-dev libusb-1.0-0-dev \
+  libbluetooth-dev libncurses-dev libmhash-dev \
+  libcurl4-openssl-dev libxi-dev libx11-dev xterm xdg-utils
+git submodule update --init --recursive
+
+# Apply the branch-maintained fixes to the pinned shared submodules.
+git -C shared/gimxhid apply ../../patches/gimxhid-pollfds-use-after-free.patch
+git -C shared/gimxusb apply ../../patches/gimxusb-pollfds-use-after-free.patch
+
+make -j2
+```
+
+After changing headers, run `make clean` before rebuilding; the legacy Makefiles do not reliably track header dependencies.
+
+
+## Windows build with MSYS2 UCRT64
+
+Use the current UCRT64 environment. Install the build tools and libraries, then clone the branch with its recursive submodules:
+
+    pacman -Syu
+    pacman -S --needed base-devel git mingw-w64-ucrt-x86_64-toolchain mingw-w64-ucrt-x86_64-wxwidgets3.2-msw mingw-w64-ucrt-x86_64-SDL2 mingw-w64-ucrt-x86_64-libxml2 mingw-w64-ucrt-x86_64-libusb mingw-w64-ucrt-x86_64-curl mingw-w64-ucrt-x86_64-ncurses mingw-w64-ucrt-x86_64-gettext mingw-w64-ucrt-x86_64-pkgconf
+    git submodule update --init --recursive
+
+Apply the maintained shared-submodule fixes, then compile:
+
+    git -C shared/gimxhid apply ../../patches/gimxhid-pollfds-use-after-free.patch
+    git -C shared/gimxusb apply ../../patches/gimxusb-pollfds-use-after-free.patch
+    git -C shared/gimxprio apply ../../patches/gimxprio-pointer-format.patch
+    git -C shared/gimxtime apply ../../patches/gimxtime-standard-printf.patch
+    git -C shared/gimxinput apply ../../patches/gimxinput-sdl2-steering-axis.patch
+    make clean
+    make -j2
+
+The GitHub Actions workflow uses the same UCRT64 packages and builds the complete application. It does not package an installer.

@@ -0,0 +1,118 @@
+# Roadmap: GIMX Reborn, PC and PS4 G29 force feedback
+
+Last updated: 2026-11-22
+
+## Scope and current branches
+
+- Application: [SounMayon/GIMX, `GIMX-Reborn`](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/tree/GIMX-Reborn)
+- Firmware: [SounMayon/GIMX-firmwares, `G29-Finished`](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX-firmwares/tree/G29-Finished/EMUG29PS4)
+- Documentation: [GIMX wiki](https://gimx.fr/wiki/index.php?title=Main_Page)
+
+## Final acceptance goal: G29 FFB on PC and PS4
+
+The project must support PC and PS4 with effects reaching a physical wheel connected through GIMX. On PC, FFB test software must discover every target effect, create/configure it, start and stop it, and produce the expected force. On PS4, retain G29 enumeration/authentication and pass through every FFB command sent by supported games so its expected force reaches the physical wheel. Merely advertising effects or preserving enumeration does not count as FFB support.
+
+Use the USB HID PID 1.0 effect set as the coverage target: constant, ramp, custom force, square, sine, triangle, sawtooth up/down, spring, damper, inertia and friction. Microsoft's GameInput HID force-feedback path aligns with PID 1.0. Logitech documents the real G29's PC-mode identity as VID/PID 046D:C24F. Before implementation, verify whether Windows test software accepts PID reports with that identity or requires Logitech's native report protocol. Test PC discovery and commands with Windows DirectInput/GameInput tools. Test PS4 compatibility separately with supported games and USB/serial captures; the PC effect list is not assumed to be generated by PS4 games.
+
+## Progress
+
+### Done
+
+- Added Ubuntu 26.04 GitHub Actions builds for the application and the `EMUG29PS4` AVR firmware.
+- Added the full recursive submodule checkout to the application build workflow.
+- Confirmed the PS4 firmware already forwards interrupt OUT reports through the adapter serial protocol (`BYTE_OUT_REPORT`); the application can consume the game's native Logitech commands without changing firmware framing.
+- Added current Linux build instructions in [BUILDING.md](BUILDING.md).
+- Baseline application build passed on Ubuntu 26.04 in [GitHub Actions run 43323280446](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/actions/runs/43323280446) at commit `d5c9407`.
+- Application build also passes with compiler/library version reporting enabled in [run 43324543826](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/actions/runs/43324543826).
+- Baseline `EMUG29PS4` firmware build passed on GitHub Actions in [run 43320703237](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX-firmwares/actions/runs/43320703237) using the existing source and vendored LUFA.
+- After that baseline, `EMUG29PS4` was moved to upstream LUFA `LUFA-170418`; the updated firmware and AVR toolchain version report pass in [run 43324588408](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX-firmwares/actions/runs/43324588408).
+- Added Linux build instructions that include the maintained patches for the pinned HID and USB submodules.
+- Registered physical G29 PC (`046d:C24F`) and PS4 (`046d:C260`) IDs with the Logitech FFB output sink so decoded constant, spring and damper effects use its native reports; build passed in [run 43337088198](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/actions/runs/43337088198).
+- Added a native-report path for timed variable and other Logitech force types the generic model cannot represent. It preserves the four source effect slots and forwards these reports to a matching physical G29; Linux build passed in [run 43337925390](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/actions/runs/43337925390), and the grouped-stop follow-up passed in [run 43338022752](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/actions/runs/43338022752).
+- Hardened the PS4 Logitech source path by rejecting truncated output reports before parsing and correcting FIFO removal to shift using `FIFO_SIZE` and clear the actual tail slot; Linux and Windows builds pass in [run 43342959350](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/actions/runs/43342959350) and [run 43342959337](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/actions/runs/43342959337).
+
+- Implemented the missing Logitech classic effects in the application haptic engine (branch `gimx-reborn-ffb`, not yet built in CI nor tested on hardware):
+  - `core/haptic/haptic_waveform.{c,h}`: pure, clock-independent evaluator for sawtooth up/down, trapezoid, rectangle, square, ramp and variable forces, computed from the elapsed time (Logitech main loop assumed to be 2 ms).
+  - `core/haptic/haptic_synth.{c,h}` and `haptic_core_update()`: sinks flagged `E_HAPTIC_SINK_CAP_SYNTHESIS` (the generic OS/SDL sink) get these effects synthesized as one constant force, summed with the constant forces of the four slots and with gain/invert tweaks applied. Other sinks are unchanged, and a physical G29 still receives the native report.
+  - `ff_lg_convert_force()`: auto-center spring (0x03) is converted to a spring and friction (0x0e) to a damper (approximations); waveform types stay `E_DATA_TYPE_LOGITECH_RAW` and carry the decoded parameters.
+  - Unit tests: `make -C core/test/haptic test` (analytic expectations, no hardware needed; the current coverage is described in the review follow-up below).
+  - Hypotheses to confirm on hardware or with captures: rectangle phase P semantics, square wave starting in the high state, sawtooth wrap-around span, ramp stopping at the target. Not covered: inertia (not part of the Logitech classic protocol), envelopes, effect direction, and a velocity-based friction model.
+
+- Review follow-up on the time based effects (`gimx-reborn-ffb`): a slot whose effect ended by itself (ramp target reached, finite square wave) is now reported back to the Logitech source (`s_haptic_source.ended`), so an identical `DOWNLOAD_AND_PLAY` or a `PLAY` restarts it instead of being ignored as "already playing". Tests now cover this lifecycle with the real Logitech source, the first wrap-around values of both sawtooth waves, the rectangle phase (offset and out of range), variable forces 0 and 2 (and the unsupported slots 1 and 3), several simultaneous waveforms with different start times, and positive/negative saturation (71 assertions). The tests check internal consistency and the documented hypotheses, not conformity to a real G29.
+- Restitution rate of synthesized effects: the waveform clock is the Logitech main loop (2 ms), but the force is only recomputed when `haptic_core_update()` runs. It runs after each received FFB report and once per controller refresh period, which is 5 ms by default for the PS4 G29 (minimum 1000 µs, see `shared/gimxcontroller/src/g29_ps4.c`). Waveforms with a period shorter than twice the refresh period are therefore aliased, and a fast square wave can be missed. Next step: measure the real send rate to the SDL sink and decide whether to average the waveform over the elapsed interval or to require a smaller refresh period.
+
+- Rate diagnostics (`gimx-reborn-ffb`): `core/haptic/haptic_stats.{c,h}` and `make HAPTIC_STATS=1` instrument the whole path (core update, synth step/result, `sink->process`, `sink->update`, `ginput_joystick_set_haptic()` with its duration and return value) with histograms (power of two buckets above 51.2 ms) and rings of requested / attempted / accepted transitions, nothing logged per event. Without `HAPTIC_STATS` the module is not built and the core has no diagnostic field. The OS sink `update()` is a no-op and all SDL work is synchronous inside `process()`; the return value of `ginput_joystick_set_haptic()` used to be ignored. Protocol and report template: `doc/FFB_rate_measurement.md`. `core/test/haptic/haptic_rate_sim` is a computation level prediction only (real synthesizer, simulated ticks, mock sink): at a 5 ms tick a square wave keeps 100 % of its transitions from a 6 ms half period, about 60 % at 4 ms and about 20 % at 2 ms; at 2 ms and 1 ms ticks all are delivered. Real SDL/OS rates still have to be measured on hardware before changing the synthesizer rate.
+
+### In progress
+
+- Finish inventorying other bundled/shared dependencies and decide whether they need independent upgrades; the current G29-only LUFA update does not change other firmware targets.
+- PC target output is confirmed: effects must reach a physical wheel connected through GIMX. Standard USB HID PID 1.0 is the first candidate for broad PC effect discovery. The real G29 is identified as 046D:C24F in PC mode, but compatibility between that identity, PID reports and Windows test software must be verified before changing firmware behavior.
+- PS4 remains a required target: preserve G29 enumeration/authentication. Current native pass-through handles source effect reports on a physical G29; the PC/CI builds cannot prove the PS4 device path or physical force. Validate it with USB/serial captures and real hardware.
+- Upstream Linux recognizes a G29 mode, but its hid-lg4ff driver exposes only FF_CONSTANT and autocenter to Linux's input force-feedback API. This describes that driver's supported interface; it does not establish the wheel hardware's full capability or Windows behavior. Use it as a reason to test the Windows protocol directly, not as a hardware capability limit. See [upstream hid-lg4ff.c](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/master/drivers/hid/hid-lg4ff.c).
+- Validate G29 behavior on hardware after the compiled LUFA update; the GitHub build cannot validate enumeration, authentication or force feedback.
+- Added a Windows UCRT64 GitHub Actions build using current MSYS2 packages; it now passes in [run 43342959337](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/actions/runs/43342959337). Bringing the legacy code up to this toolchain required downstream portability fixes for GCC 16 pointer/time formats, SDL2's `SDL_HAPTIC_STEERING_AXIS`, and wxWidgets 3.2 static command-line descriptors, plus an explicit downloader integer type. The run used GCC/G++ 16.2.0, wxWidgets 3.2.11, SDL2 2.32.10, libxml2 2.15.4, libusb 1.0.30, libcurl 8.23.0, ncurses 6.6 and gettext 1.0. MSYS2 is rolling, so CI records exact package versions on every run. See [SDL2 steering axis](https://wiki.libsdl.org/SDL2/SDL_HAPTIC_STEERING_AXIS).
+- Keep protocol and firmware behavior changes separate from dependency updates so each step can be compiled and reviewed.
+
+## Audit notes
+
+### Application and dependencies
+
+The project is a C/C++ Makefile build with shared Git submodules. System dependencies include wxWidgets, SDL2, libxml2, libcurl, BlueZ, ncurses, mhash, libusb, X11/Xi and gettext. The Ubuntu 26.04 workflow resolves package versions from the current Ubuntu repositories.
+
+The successful baseline exposed and fixed these issues under the current compiler and libraries:
+- The pinned `gimxhid` and `gimxusb` submodules freed libusb poll descriptors before rollback accessed them. The fixes are maintained in this repository under `patches/` and applied by CI and the documented Linux build.
+- The root Makefile did not make `build-loader` depend on `build-shared`, allowing parallel linking before `gimxtime` existed.
+- Linux depended on the removed `ncursesw5-config` executable. The core Makefile now supports ncursesw5, ncursesw6 and pkg-config.
+- Current compiler diagnostics exposed missing standard-library declarations and mismatched/no-prototype Bluetooth APIs; the affected includes, declarations and call sites are corrected.
+
+The Ubuntu 26.04 Linux application build is green. The version-reporting step recorded GCC/G++ 15.2.0, wxWidgets 3.2.9, SDL2 2.32.10, libxml2 2.15.2, libusb 1.0.29, ncursesw 6.6, libcurl 8.18.0 and BlueZ 5.85. Current UCRT64 build passes on [run 43342959337](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/actions/runs/43342959337) with GCC 16.2.0, wxWidgets 3.2.11, SDL2 2.32.10, libxml2 2.15.4, libusb 1.0.30, libcurl 8.23.0 and ncurses 6.6. It exercises the downstream portability fixes for the pinned Windows priority/time/input submodules and the three wxWidgets command-line descriptors. The Linux build for the same code is green on [run 43342959350](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/actions/runs/43342959350).
+
+The AVR workflow recorded avr-gcc 14.3.0, avr-libc 2.2.1, binutils-avr 2.43.50 and make 4.4.1.
+
+### PC USB PID gap
+
+- [Descriptors.c](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX-firmwares/blob/G29-Finished/EMUG29PS4/Descriptors.c) advertises Generic Desktop, Button and Logitech vendor pages. The report descriptor has no PID Usage Page `0x0F` or standard Set Effect/Periodic/Condition report collections, so it does not currently advertise standard HID PID effects to a PC.
+- Microsoft documents GameInput force feedback over HID as aligned with USB PID 1.0. Logitech's G29 product specification identifies `046D:C24F` as PC mode. These facts support a standard PID implementation as the first design candidate, but do not prove that Windows will route every test application through PID when using the Logitech identity. Verify enumeration and effect creation on Windows before settling descriptor identity and protocol. References: [Microsoft GameInput FFB](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/gaming/gdk/docs/features/common/input/hardware/input-hardware-force-feedback?view=gdk-2604), [Logitech G29 specifications](https://support.logi.com/hc/en-us/articles/360023463753-G29-Driving-Force-Racing-Wheel-Technical-Specifications).
+- [emu.c](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX-firmwares/blob/G29-Finished/EMUG29PS4/emu.c) handles the existing vendor feature IDs `F0-F3`; it does not allocate PID effect blocks or process standard PID output reports.
+- The application haptic core currently models rumble, constant, spring and damper (plus LEDs/range). The Logitech conversion supports only constant, a limited variable-to-constant case, spring, damper and high-resolution spring/damper. Ramp, periodic waveforms, custom force, inertia and friction are not represented in the generic core.
+- The generic application model and OS/SDL sink still expose only rumble, constant, spring and damper; there is no general periodic scheduler or motion-aware inertia/friction model. For the PS4 G29 source, unmodeled Logitech reports now bypass that model only when the output is a matching physical G29. Other physical wheels still require explicit effect conversion or synthesis.
+- PC support therefore requires coordinated firmware descriptor/report handling, an expanded effect model, parameter-block lifecycle and effect execution, plus a defined route to the physical wheel. Keep the PS4 vendor protocol as a separate regression path and compile both targets after each step.
+- Protocol decision checkpoint: test standard HID PID enumeration and all target effect creation on Windows using the intended PC identity. If the Logitech identity selects a native-only path, compare a generic PID identity and the real G29 descriptor/report capture before choosing a compatible architecture.
+- USB-IF references: [PID Class Definition 1.0](https://www.usb.org/sites/default/files/documents/pid1_01.pdf) and [HID Usage Tables](https://www.usb.org/sites/default/files/hut1_3_0.pdf).
+
+### Odrive-Wheel as the DIY physical FFB output
+
+- The [Odrive-Wheel `feature/t300-hef4021` firmware](https://github.com/SounMayon/Odrive-Wheel/tree/feature/t300-hef4021) uses USB VID/PID `1209:0D40` and exposes a composite HID FFB plus CDC device. Its USB identity is not the Logitech G29 identity used by the PS4 emulator.
+- Its HID FFB implementation handles effect creation, constant, ramp, periodic and condition reports, and its effects calculator renders periodic waveforms and position/velocity/acceleration-based forces. Custom-force and sample-download reports currently have no implementation, so complete HID PID coverage is not established.
+- GIMX's generic OS sink advertises only rumble, constant, spring and damper. It warns and drops `LOGITECH_RAW`; the Logitech sink forwards native reports only for Logitech G29 output IDs. Therefore the current GIMX branch cannot deliver every PS4-native G29 report to this Odrive device. Some modeled effects may work through the OS haptic API, but that path has not been verified with this firmware.
+- Required integration: add and validate a dedicated `1209:0D40` output path that converts the G29 source reports into effects the Odrive HID PID endpoint accepts, including update/play/stop, timing, gain and effect-slot lifecycle. Preserve direct G29 pass-through as a separate path.
+- On a PC, the Odrive firmware presents its own HID PID identity; actual FFB use depends on host/game support. Flashing `EMUG29PS4.hex` onto the separate GIMX adapter does not change the Odrive USB identity. The PS4 emulator descriptor and Odrive output device are different USB devices and roles.
+
+### Existing G29 PS4 emulation
+
+- The firmware descriptor advertises Logitech `046d:C260` and interrupt input/output endpoints in [Descriptors.c](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX-firmwares/blob/G29-Finished/EMUG29PS4/Descriptors.c#L95-L105).
+- The firmware handles HID feature requests `F1/F2` in [emu.c](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX-firmwares/blob/G29-Finished/EMUG29PS4/emu.c#L32-L45), sends a serial header and spins until `spoofReply` arrives. There is no timeout or recovery path, so a lost reply can stall that request.
+- The application already registers a G29 PS4 controller, builds wheel/pedal/button reports, handles authentication and sends reports at a 5 ms default interval.
+- FFB is not universally flattened to constant: the Logitech parser maps `CONSTANT`, `SPRING` and `DAMPER` to distinct core types; simple `VARIABLE` forces are approximated as constant. Timed `VARIABLE` and other Logitech force types now retain their original report for native pass-through to a physical G29. For other wheel models, unknown reports still are not translated. See [the conversions](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/blob/GIMX-Reborn/core/haptic/common/ff_lg.c#L385-L497) and [Logitech report encoding](https://github.com/SounMayon/GIMX/blob/GIMX-Reborn/core/haptic/common/ff_lg.c#L550-L655).
+- GIMX's guide documents working wheel, pedal, wheel-button and shifter controls, combined input devices, and force feedback; the GIMX path is an existing G29 emulator that needs validation and robustness work. See the [G29 to PS4 guide](https://gimx.fr/wiki/index.php?connectiontype=USB&device=SteeringWheel&ostype=windows&platform=ps4&title=Guide) and [force feedback notes](https://gimx.fr/wiki/index.php?title=Force_Feedback).
+- Highest-value additions: bound control/serial lengths, add a timeout and recoverable error path for `F1/F2`, test report boundaries and reconnect behavior, compare descriptors/reports with a genuine G29 capture, then validate authentication and force feedback on a real G29, adapter and PS4. Do not change framing or timing without captures and hardware validation.
+- Firmware baseline used bundled LUFA `130901`. After both baseline builds passed, only the G29 target was switched to the pinned upstream `LUFA-170418` submodule. Its build passes with the descriptor callback updated to LUFA's `uint16_t wIndex` signature; other firmware targets still use the existing shared LUFA tree.
+
+## Next steps
+
+1. Preserve the green Linux, Windows/UCRT64 and AVR baselines; keep the current CI results as the compile checkpoint before protocol or firmware changes.
+2. Resolve PC protocol compatibility on Windows: test the target PC identity and the real G29's descriptor/report behavior, then choose standard HID PID, native Logitech reports or a measured combination. Record the test application and observable capabilities.
+3. Specify the selected PC descriptor/report IDs, effect pool strategy, capabilities and route to physical output against USB-IF PID 1.0 and the GIMX haptic sink.
+4. Extend the shared haptic model and firmware/application transport for every PC target effect; implement effect allocation, parameter updates, gain/envelopes, operation/status and motion-aware condition effects.
+5. Validate PS4 FFB delivery with reports captured from supported games and a physical G29; extend translation for non-G29 physical wheel sinks so timed and periodic effects are rendered where supported.
+6. Build a Windows test matrix that enumerates each PC target effect, creates/configures it, starts/stops it and checks the resulting force. Retain the PS4 firmware/application paths as regression checks.
+7. Validate PC and PS4 descriptors/reports with USB captures, then verify PC target effects and supported PS4 game effects on the physical wheel.
+8. Update this roadmap after every implementation step and CI result.
+

+                }

+        }

+// decode the time based Logitech classic forces, see chapter 3.2 of the Logitech protocol specification
+static void ff_lg_decode_waveform(uint8_t slot_index, const s_ff_lg_command * force, s_haptic_waveform * wf) {
+
+    const unsigned char * p = force->parameters;
+    memset(wf, 0x00, sizeof(*wf));
+    switch (force->force_type) {
+    case FF_LG_FTYPE_SAWTOOTH_UP:
+    case FF_LG_FTYPE_SAWTOOTH_DOWN:
+        wf->kind = (force->force_type == FF_LG_FTYPE_SAWTOOTH_UP) ? E_WAVEFORM_SAWTOOTH_UP : E_WAVEFORM_SAWTOOTH_DOWN;
+        wf->l1 = p[0];
+        wf->l2 = p[1];
+        wf->l0 = p[2];
+        wf->t3 = p[4] >> 4;
+        wf->step = p[4] & 0x0f;
+        break;
+    case FF_LG_FTYPE_TRAPEZOID:
+        wf->kind = E_WAVEFORM_TRAPEZOID;
+        wf->l1 = p[0];
+        wf->l2 = p[1];
+        wf->t1 = p[2];
+        wf->t2 = p[3];
+        wf->t3 = p[4] >> 4;
+        wf->step = p[4] & 0x0f;
+        break;
+    case FF_LG_FTYPE_RECTANGLE:
+        wf->kind = E_WAVEFORM_RECTANGLE;
+        wf->l1 = p[0];
+        wf->l2 = p[1];
+        wf->t1 = p[2];
+        wf->t2 = p[3];
+        wf->phase = p[4];
+        break;
+    case FF_LG_FTYPE_SQUARE_WAVE:
+        wf->kind = E_WAVEFORM_SQUARE;
+        wf->amplitude = p[0];
+        wf->half_period = (uint16_t)(p[1] | (p[2] << 8));
+        wf->periods = p[3];
+        break;
+    case FF_LG_FTYPE_RAMP:
+        wf->kind = E_WAVEFORM_RAMP;
+        wf->l1 = p[0];
+        wf->l2 = p[1];
+        wf->direction = p[2] & 0x01;
+        wf->t3 = p[3] >> 4;
+        wf->step = p[3] & 0x0f;
+        break;
+    case FF_LG_FTYPE_VARIABLE:
+        // only forces 0 and 2 are defined for this type
+        wf->kind = E_WAVEFORM_VARIABLE;
+        if (slot_index == 2) {
+            wf->l1 = p[1];
+            wf->t3 = p[3] >> 4;
+            wf->step = p[3] & 0x0f;
+            wf->direction = (p[4] >> 4) & 0x01;
+        } else {
+            wf->l1 = p[0];
+            wf->t3 = p[2] >> 4;
+            wf->step = p[2] & 0x0f;
+            wf->direction = p[4] & 0x01;
+        }
+        break;
+    default:
+        break;
+    }
+}

+    uint64_t now = (uint64_t)gtime_gettime();
+

+#ifdef HAPTIC_STATS
+    if (core->report_pending) {
+        HSTAT_EVENT(HSTAT_CORE_UPDATE_REPORT, now);
+        core->report_pending = 0;
+    }
+#endif
+    HSTAT_REPORT(now);
+
     s_haptic_core_data data;
     while (core->source.ptr->get(core->source.state, &data)) {
         haptic_tweak_apply(&core->tweaks, &data);
+        if (synthesis && haptic_synth_feed(&core->synth, &data, now)) {
+            continue;
+        }
+        HSTAT_EVENT(HSTAT_SINK_PROCESS, now);
         core->sink.ptr->process(core->sink.state, &data);
     }
 
+    if (synthesis) {
+        int send = haptic_synth_step(&core->synth, now, &core->tweaks, &data);
+        HSTAT_EVENT(HSTAT_SYNTH_STEP, now);
+
+        // keep the source slots in sync with the effects that ended by themselves
+        unsigned int ended = haptic_synth_take_ended(&core->synth);
+        if (ended != 0 && core->source.ptr->ended != NULL) {
+            unsigned int slot;
+            for (slot = 0; slot < HAPTIC_SYNTH_SLOTS; ++slot) {
+                if (ended & (1U << slot)) {
+                    core->source.ptr->ended(core->source.state, slot);
+                }
+            }
+        }
+
+        if (send) {
+            HSTAT_EVENT(HSTAT_SYNTH_RESULT, now);
+            HSTAT_LEVEL(HSTAT_SYNTH_RESULT, now, data.constant.level);
+            HSTAT_EVENT(HSTAT_SINK_PROCESS, now);
+            // the constant force already includes the tweaks
+            core->sink.ptr->process(core->sink.state, &data);
+        }
+    }
+
+    HSTAT_EVENT(HSTAT_SINK_UPDATE, now);
     core->sink.ptr->update(core->sink.state);
 }

new file mode 100644

+/*
+ License: GPLv3
+ */
+
+#include <string.h>
+#include <haptic/haptic_stats.h>
+
+s_haptic_stats haptic_stats;
+
+static const char * const point_names[HSTAT_POINTS] = {
+    "haptic_core_update (all)",
+    "haptic_core_update (after report)",
+    "haptic_synth_step",
+    "synth result requested",
+    "sink->process",
+    "sink->update",
+    "SDL call attempted",
+    "SDL call accepted",
+};
+
+void haptic_stats_reset(void) {
+
+    memset(&haptic_stats, 0x00, sizeof(haptic_stats));
+}
+
+static void series_add_interval(s_haptic_stat_series * s, uint64_t interval) {
+
+    if (s->count == 0 || interval < s->min_ns) {
+        s->min_ns = interval;
+    }
+    if (interval > s->max_ns) {
+        s->max_ns = interval;
+    }
+    s->sum_ns += interval;
+    if (interval > 1000000ULL) ++s->gt1ms;
+    if (interval > 2000000ULL) ++s->gt2ms;
+    if (interval > 5000000ULL) ++s->gt5ms;
+    if (interval > 10000000ULL) ++s->gt10ms;
+    uint64_t bucket = interval / HSTAT_BUCKET_NS;
+    if (bucket >= HSTAT_BUCKETS) {
+        ++s->overflow;
+        unsigned int k = 0;
+        uint64_t limit = 2 * HSTAT_BUCKETS * HSTAT_BUCKET_NS; // upper bound of the first overflow bucket
+        while (k < HSTAT_OVERFLOW_BUCKETS - 1 && interval >= limit) {
+            ++k;
+            limit *= 2;
+        }
+        ++s->overflow_log[k];
+    } else {
+        ++s->buckets[bucket];
+    }
+    ++s->count; // number of intervals
+}
+
+static void series_event(s_haptic_stat_series * s, uint64_t now) {
+
+    if (s->last_ns != 0 && now >= s->last_ns) {
+        series_add_interval(s, now - s->last_ns);
+    }
+    s->last_ns = now;
+}
+
+void haptic_stats_event(e_haptic_stat_point point, uint64_t now_ns) {
+
+    if ((unsigned int)point < HSTAT_POINTS) {
+        series_event(haptic_stats.points + point, now_ns);
+    }
+}
+
+static void level_record(uint64_t now, int level, uint8_t * valid, int32_t * last, uint64_t * changes, uint64_t * unchanged,
+        s_haptic_stat_transition * ring, uint64_t * n) {
+
+    if (*valid && *last == level) {
+        ++*unchanged;
+        return;
+    }
+    if (*valid) {
+        ++*changes;
+    }
+    *valid = 1;
+    *last = level;
+    ring[*n % HSTAT_RING].ns = now;
+    ring[*n % HSTAT_RING].level = level;
+    ++*n;
+}
+
+void haptic_stats_level(e_haptic_stat_point point, uint64_t now_ns, int level) {
+
+    s_haptic_stats * s = &haptic_stats;
+    if (point == HSTAT_SYNTH_RESULT) {
+        level_record(now_ns, level, &s->requested_valid, &s->requested_last, &s->requested_changes, &s->requested_unchanged,
+                s->requested, &s->requested_n);
+    }
+}
+
+void haptic_stats_sdl_call(uint64_t start_ns, uint64_t end_ns, int ret, int constant, int level) {
+
+    s_haptic_stats * s = &haptic_stats;
+    series_event(s->points + HSTAT_SDL_CALL, start_ns);
+    if (end_ns >= start_ns) {
+        series_add_interval(&s->sdl_duration, end_ns - start_ns);
+    }
+    if (constant) {
+        level_record(start_ns, level, &s->attempted_valid, &s->attempted_last, &s->attempted_changes, &s->attempted_unchanged,
+                s->attempted, &s->attempted_n);
+    } else {
+        ++s->sdl_non_constant;
+    }
+    if (ret < 0) {
+        ++s->sdl_errors;
+        return;
+    }
+    series_event(s->points + HSTAT_SDL_ACCEPTED, start_ns);
+    if (constant) {
+        level_record(start_ns, level, &s->accepted_valid, &s->accepted_last, &s->accepted_changes, &s->accepted_unchanged,
+                s->accepted, &s->accepted_n);
+    }
+}
+
+uint64_t haptic_stats_percentile_ns(const s_haptic_stat_series * series, unsigned int percentile) {
+
+    if (series->count == 0) {
+        return 0;
+    }
+    uint64_t target = (series->count * percentile + 99) / 100; // rank, rounded up
+    if (target == 0) {
+        target = 1;
+    }
+    uint64_t seen = 0;
+    unsigned int i;
+    for (i = 0; i < HSTAT_BUCKETS; ++i) {
+        seen += series->buckets[i];
+        if (seen >= target) {
+            return (uint64_t)(i + 1) * HSTAT_BUCKET_NS;
+        }
+    }
+    // above the histogram: power of two buckets, the value is the upper bound of the bucket, never above the real maximum
+    uint64_t upper = 2 * HSTAT_BUCKETS * HSTAT_BUCKET_NS;
+    for (i = 0; i < HSTAT_OVERFLOW_BUCKETS; ++i) {
+        seen += series->overflow_log[i];
+        if (seen >= target) {
+            return (i == HSTAT_OVERFLOW_BUCKETS - 1 || upper > series->max_ns) ? series->max_ns : upper;
+        }
+        upper *= 2;
+    }
+    return series->max_ns;
+}
+
+uint64_t haptic_stats_mean_ns(const s_haptic_stat_series * series) {
+
+    return series->count ? series->sum_ns / series->count : 0;
+}
+
+static void dump_series(FILE * out, const char * name, const s_haptic_stat_series * s) {
+
+    if (s->count == 0) {
+        fprintf(out, "  %-34s no interval recorded\n", name);
+        return;
+    }
+    uint64_t mean = haptic_stats_mean_ns(s);
+    fprintf(out, "  %-34s n=%-8llu mean=%7.3f ms (%7.1f Hz) median<=%6.2f p95<=%6.2f p99<=%6.2f min=%6.3f max=%7.3f ms  >1ms:%llu >2ms:%llu >5ms:%llu >10ms:%llu\n",
+            name, (unsigned long long)s->count, mean / 1e6, mean ? 1e9 / mean : 0.0,
+            haptic_stats_percentile_ns(s, 50) / 1e6, haptic_stats_percentile_ns(s, 95) / 1e6, haptic_stats_percentile_ns(s, 99) / 1e6,
+            s->min_ns / 1e6, s->max_ns / 1e6,
+            (unsigned long long)s->gt1ms, (unsigned long long)s->gt2ms, (unsigned long long)s->gt5ms, (unsigned long long)s->gt10ms);
+}
+
+static void dump_ring(FILE * out, const char * name, const s_haptic_stat_transition * ring, uint64_t n) {
+
+    uint64_t count = n < HSTAT_RING ? n : HSTAT_RING;
+    uint64_t i;
+    fprintf(out, "  %s (%llu most recent shown of %llu, [time in ms modulo 1000 s: level]):", name, (unsigned long long)(count < 24 ? count : 24), (unsigned long long)n);
+    for (i = 0; i < count && i < 24; ++i) { // the 24 most recent in chronological order
+        uint64_t idx = n - (count < 24 ? count : 24) + i;
+        fprintf(out, " [%.3f ms:%d]", (ring[idx % HSTAT_RING].ns % 1000000000000ULL) / 1e6, (int)ring[idx % HSTAT_RING].level);
+    }
+    fprintf(out, "\n");
+}
+
+void haptic_stats_dump(FILE * out, int with_transitions) {
+
+    const s_haptic_stats * s = &haptic_stats;
+    int i;
+    fprintf(out, "haptic stats: intervals between events\n");
+    for (i = 0; i < HSTAT_POINTS; ++i) {
+        dump_series(out, point_names[i], s->points + i);
+    }
+    dump_series(out, "SDL call duration", &s->sdl_duration);
+    fprintf(out, "  SDL errors=%llu, SDL calls that were not a constant force=%llu\n",
+            (unsigned long long)s->sdl_errors, (unsigned long long)s->sdl_non_constant);
+    fprintf(out, "  constant level, requested by the synthesizer: %llu changes, %llu unchanged\n",
+            (unsigned long long)s->requested_changes, (unsigned long long)s->requested_unchanged);
+    fprintf(out, "  constant level, attempted (passed to SDL): %llu changes, %llu unchanged; accepted by SDL: %llu changes, %llu unchanged\n",
+            (unsigned long long)s->attempted_changes, (unsigned long long)s->attempted_unchanged,
+            (unsigned long long)s->accepted_changes, (unsigned long long)s->accepted_unchanged);
+    if (with_transitions) {
+        dump_ring(out, "requested transitions", s->requested, s->requested_n);
+        dump_ring(out, "attempted transitions", s->attempted, s->attempted_n);
+        dump_ring(out, "accepted transitions", s->accepted, s->accepted_n);
+    }
+}
+
+void haptic_stats_report_if_due(FILE * out, uint64_t now_ns, uint64_t period_ns) {
+
+    if (haptic_stats.last_report_ns == 0) {
+        haptic_stats.last_report_ns = now_ns;
+    } else if (now_ns - haptic_stats.last_report_ns >= period_ns) {
+        haptic_stats.last_report_ns = now_ns;
+        haptic_stats_dump(out, 0);
+    }
+}
diff --git a/core/haptic/haptic_stats.h b/core/haptic/haptic_stats.h
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..a23df11
--- /dev/null
+++ b/core/haptic/haptic_stats.h
@@ -0,0 +1,111 @@
+/*
+ License: GPLv3
+ */
+
+/*
+ * Diagnostic statistics for the force feedback path, to separate:
+ *  - the computation rate: how often haptic_core_update() runs and recomputes the synthesized force,
+ *  - the restitution rate: how often the sink really receives a new force and calls into SDL.
+ *
+ * Nothing is logged per event: intervals go into histograms, transitions into fixed-size rings, and the summary
+ * is printed every few seconds and when the core is cleaned. The clock is provided by the caller (nanoseconds).
+ *
+ * The instrumentation is compiled in only with -DHAPTIC_STATS (make HAPTIC_STATS=1). Without it the macros below are
+ * empty, haptic_stats.c is not part of the core build and the core keeps no diagnostic field. The unit tests build
+ * haptic_stats.c on their own. The counters are not thread safe: they are meant for diagnostics.
+ */
+
+#ifndef HAPTIC_STATS_H_
+#define HAPTIC_STATS_H_
+
+#include <stdint.h>
+#include <stdio.h>
+
+typedef enum {
+    HSTAT_CORE_UPDATE,        // entry of haptic_core_update(), any caller
+    HSTAT_CORE_UPDATE_REPORT, // entry of haptic_core_update() right after an FFB report was received
+    HSTAT_SYNTH_STEP,         // haptic_synth_step() computations
+    HSTAT_SYNTH_RESULT,       // haptic_synth_step() results to send to the sink (requested level)
+    HSTAT_SINK_PROCESS,       // haptic_sink->process() calls, any type
+    HSTAT_SINK_UPDATE,        // haptic_sink->update() calls
+    HSTAT_SDL_CALL,           // ginput_joystick_set_haptic() calls attempted (synchronous down to SDL)
+    HSTAT_SDL_ACCEPTED,       // the same calls, only those that returned a non negative value
+    HSTAT_POINTS
+} e_haptic_stat_point;
+
+#define HSTAT_BUCKET_NS 100000ULL // 100 us per histogram bucket
+#define HSTAT_BUCKETS 512         // up to 51.2 ms, then overflow
+#define HSTAT_OVERFLOW_BUCKETS 8 // above the histogram: [51.2 ms * 2^k, 51.2 ms * 2^(k+1)), the last one is open ended
+#define HSTAT_RING 256
+
+typedef struct {
+    uint64_t count;
+    uint64_t last_ns;
+    uint64_t min_ns, max_ns, sum_ns; // intervals between consecutive events (durations for the SDL duration series)
+    uint64_t gt1ms, gt2ms, gt5ms, gt10ms;
+    uint32_t buckets[HSTAT_BUCKETS];
+    uint32_t overflow;                            // total number of intervals above the histogram
+    uint32_t overflow_log[HSTAT_OVERFLOW_BUCKETS]; // the same, in power of two buckets
+} s_haptic_stat_series;
+
+typedef struct {
+    uint64_t ns;
+    int32_t level;
+} s_haptic_stat_transition;
+
+typedef struct {
+    s_haptic_stat_series points[HSTAT_POINTS];
+    s_haptic_stat_series sdl_duration; // duration of each ginput_joystick_set_haptic() call (software submission only)
+    uint64_t sdl_errors;               // calls that returned a negative value
+    uint64_t sdl_non_constant;         // calls that were not a constant force (spring, damper)
+    uint64_t requested_changes, requested_unchanged; // constant level requested by the synthesizer
+    uint64_t attempted_changes, attempted_unchanged; // constant level passed to SDL, whatever the result
+    uint64_t accepted_changes, accepted_unchanged;   // constant level passed to SDL and accepted (return value >= 0)
+    int32_t requested_last, attempted_last, accepted_last;
+    uint8_t requested_valid, attempted_valid, accepted_valid;
+    s_haptic_stat_transition requested[HSTAT_RING], attempted[HSTAT_RING], accepted[HSTAT_RING];
+    uint64_t requested_n, attempted_n, accepted_n; // total number of transitions recorded (ring index = n % HSTAT_RING)
+    uint64_t last_report_ns;
+} s_haptic_stats;
+
+extern s_haptic_stats haptic_stats;
+
+void haptic_stats_reset(void);
+
+// record one occurrence of a point
+void haptic_stats_event(e_haptic_stat_point point, uint64_t now_ns);
+
+// record the constant force level requested by the synthesizer (point must be HSTAT_SYNTH_RESULT)
+void haptic_stats_level(e_haptic_stat_point point, uint64_t now_ns, int level);
+
+// record one SDL call: attempted always, accepted only if ret >= 0. The level is only used if constant is not 0.
+// The duration is the software submission time (up to the driver), not the physical reaction of the wheel.
+void haptic_stats_sdl_call(uint64_t start_ns, uint64_t end_ns, int ret, int constant, int level);
+
+// percentile (0-100) of the intervals of a series, resolution = HSTAT_BUCKET_NS, upper bound of the bucket
+uint64_t haptic_stats_percentile_ns(const s_haptic_stat_series * series, unsigned int percentile);
+
+// mean interval in ns (0 if fewer than 2 events)
+uint64_t haptic_stats_mean_ns(const s_haptic_stat_series * series);
+
+// print the summary
+void haptic_stats_dump(FILE * out, int with_transitions);
+
+// print the summary if at least period_ns elapsed since the last report
+void haptic_stats_report_if_due(FILE * out, uint64_t now_ns, uint64_t period_ns);
+
+#ifdef HAPTIC_STATS
+#define HSTAT_EVENT(POINT, NOW) haptic_stats_event(POINT, NOW)
+#define HSTAT_LEVEL(POINT, NOW, LEVEL) haptic_stats_level(POINT, NOW, LEVEL)
+#define HSTAT_SDL(START, END, RET, CONSTANT, LEVEL) haptic_stats_sdl_call(START, END, RET, CONSTANT, LEVEL)
+#define HSTAT_REPORT(NOW) haptic_stats_report_if_due(stderr, NOW, 5000000000ULL)
+#define HSTAT_DUMP() haptic_stats_dump(stderr, 1)
+#else
+#define HSTAT_EVENT(POINT, NOW) do { } while (0)
+#define HSTAT_LEVEL(POINT, NOW, LEVEL) do { } while (0)
+#define HSTAT_SDL(START, END, RET, CONSTANT, LEVEL) do { } while (0)
+#define HSTAT_REPORT(NOW) do { } while (0)
+#define HSTAT_DUMP() do { } while (0)
+#endif
+
+#endif /* HAPTIC_STATS_H_ */

new file mode 100644
index 0000000..bab15de
--- /dev/null
+++ b/core/haptic/haptic_synth.c
@@ -0,0 +1,127 @@
+/*
+ License: GPLv3
+ */
+
+#include <string.h>
+#include <limits.h>
+#include <haptic/haptic_synth.h>
+#include <haptic/haptic_tweaks.h>
+
+void haptic_synth_init(s_haptic_synth * synth) {
+
+    memset(synth, 0x00, sizeof(*synth));
+}
+
+static int any_active(const s_haptic_synth * synth) {
+    int i;
+    for (i = 0; i < HAPTIC_SYNTH_SLOTS; ++i) {
+        if (synth->active[i]) {
+            return 1;
+        }
+    }
+    return 0;
+}
+
+int haptic_synth_feed(s_haptic_synth * synth, const s_haptic_core_data * data, uint64_t now_ns) {
+
+    if (!data->source_slot_valid || data->source_slot >= HAPTIC_SYNTH_SLOTS) {
+        return 0;
+    }
+
+    unsigned int slot = data->source_slot;
+
+    if (data->waveform_valid) {
+        if (data->playing) {
+            // a new start, or new parameters: restart the effect
+            if (!synth->active[slot] || memcmp(&synth->wf[slot], &data->waveform, sizeof(data->waveform))) {
+                synth->wf[slot] = data->waveform;
+                synth->start_ns[slot] = now_ns;
+            }
+            synth->active[slot] = 1;
+        } else {
+            synth->active[slot] = 0;
+        }
+        synth->dirty = 1;
+        return 1;
+    }
+
+    if (data->type == E_DATA_TYPE_CONSTANT) {
+        synth->constant[slot] = data->playing ? data->constant.level : 0;
+        synth->constant_playing[slot] = data->playing;
+        if (synth->on || any_active(synth)) {
+            synth->dirty = 1;
+            return 1;
+        }
+        return 0; // no time based effect: let it go through unchanged
+    }
+
+    return 0;
+}
+
+int haptic_synth_step(s_haptic_synth * synth, uint64_t now_ns, const s_haptic_core_tweaks * tweaks, s_haptic_core_data * out) {
+
+    unsigned int i;
+    int32_t sum = 0;
+
+    for (i = 0; i < HAPTIC_SYNTH_SLOTS; ++i) {
+        if (synth->active[i]) {
+            int finished = 0;
+            uint64_t elapsed = now_ns > synth->start_ns[i] ? now_ns - synth->start_ns[i] : 0;
+            int16_t level = haptic_waveform_eval(synth->wf + i, elapsed, &finished);
+            if (finished) {
+                synth->active[i] = 0;
+                synth->ended_mask |= (1U << i);
+                synth->dirty = 1;
+                continue;
+            }
+            // apply the constant force tweaks (gain, invert) to the waveform
+            s_haptic_core_data tmp;
+            memset(&tmp, 0x00, sizeof(tmp));
+            tmp.type = E_DATA_TYPE_CONSTANT;
+            tmp.playing = 1;
+            tmp.constant.level = level;
+            haptic_tweak_apply(tweaks, &tmp);
+            sum += tmp.constant.level;
+        }
+    }
+
+    int now_on = any_active(synth);
+
+    if (!now_on && !synth->on) {
+        synth->dirty = 0;
+        return 0;
+    }
+
+    for (i = 0; i < HAPTIC_SYNTH_SLOTS; ++i) {
+        if (synth->constant_playing[i]) {
+            sum += synth->constant[i];
+        }
+    }
+    if (sum > SHRT_MAX) {
+        sum = SHRT_MAX;
+    } else if (sum < -SHRT_MAX) {
+        sum = -SHRT_MAX;
+    }
+
+    int changed = (sum != synth->last_level) || (now_on != synth->on) || synth->dirty;
+    synth->on = now_on;
+    synth->dirty = 0;
+
+    if (!changed) {
+        return 0;
+    }
+    synth->last_level = sum;
+
+    memset(out, 0x00, sizeof(*out));
+    out->type = E_DATA_TYPE_CONSTANT;
+    out->playing = 1;
+    out->constant.level = (int16_t)sum;
+    return 1;
+}
+
+unsigned int haptic_synth_take_ended(s_haptic_synth * synth) {
+
+    unsigned int mask = synth->ended_mask;
+    synth->ended_mask = 0;
+    return mask;
+}

+/*
+ License: GPLv3
+ */
+
+/*
+ * Software synthesis of time based effects for sinks that only have a constant force channel.
+ *
+ * The 4 Logitech force slots are tracked. While at least one time based effect is playing, the constant forces
+ * and the waveforms of all the slots are summed into a single constant force. The time is provided by the caller.
+ */
+
+#ifndef HAPTIC_SYNTH_H_
+#define HAPTIC_SYNTH_H_
+
+#include <stdint.h>
+#include "haptic_core.h"
+
+#define HAPTIC_SYNTH_SLOTS 4
+
+typedef struct {
+    s_haptic_waveform wf[HAPTIC_SYNTH_SLOTS];
+    uint8_t active[HAPTIC_SYNTH_SLOTS];
+    uint64_t start_ns[HAPTIC_SYNTH_SLOTS];
+    int16_t constant[HAPTIC_SYNTH_SLOTS];
+    uint8_t constant_playing[HAPTIC_SYNTH_SLOTS];
+    uint8_t on;
+    uint8_t dirty;
+    uint8_t ended_mask; // slots whose effect ended by itself since the last call to haptic_synth_take_ended()
+    int32_t last_level;
+} s_haptic_synth;
+
+void haptic_synth_init(s_haptic_synth * synth);
+
+/*
+ * Offer a decoded data to the synthesizer.
+ * Returns 1 if the data was consumed (the caller must not send it to the sink), 0 otherwise.
+ */
+int haptic_synth_feed(s_haptic_synth * synth, const s_haptic_core_data * data, uint64_t now_ns);
+
+/*
+ * Compute the constant force to send to the sink.
+ * Returns 1 if out has to be sent to the sink, 0 if nothing changed.
+ */
+int haptic_synth_step(s_haptic_synth * synth, uint64_t now_ns, const s_haptic_core_tweaks * tweaks, s_haptic_core_data * out);
+
+/*
+ * Get (and clear) the mask of the slots whose effect ended by itself (ramp target reached, square periods played).
+ * The source has to be told, otherwise it would still consider these slots as playing and ignore an identical replay.
+ */
+unsigned int haptic_synth_take_ended(s_haptic_synth * synth);
+
+#endif /* HAPTIC_SYNTH_H_ */

             APPLY_GAIN(data->damper.coefficient.left, tweaks->gain.damper, -SHRT_MAX, SHRT_MAX)
             APPLY_GAIN(data->damper.coefficient.right, tweaks->gain.damper, -SHRT_MAX, SHRT_MAX)
         }
         if (tweaks->invert) {
+            data->logitech_report_valid = 0;
             SWAP(int16_t, data->spring.coefficient.left, data->spring.coefficient.right)
         }
         break;
+    case E_DATA_TYPE_LOGITECH_RAW:
+        // Native reports are forwarded byte-for-byte to a matching G29 sink.
+        break;
     case E_DATA_TYPE_LEDS:
         break;
     case E_DATA_TYPE_RANGE:
diff --git a/core/haptic/haptic_waveform.c b/core/haptic/haptic_waveform.c
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..9d43d60
--- /dev/null
+++ b/core/haptic/haptic_waveform.c
@@ -0,0 +1,175 @@
+/*
+ License: GPLv3
+ */
+
+#include <stddef.h>
+#include <limits.h>
+#include <haptic/haptic_waveform.h>
+
+// same conversion as ff_lg_u8_to_s16 (127 and 128 are the center positions)
+static int16_t level_to_s16(int level) {
+    int c = level;
+    if (c < 0) {
+        c = 0;
+    } else if (c > UCHAR_MAX) {
+        c = UCHAR_MAX;
+    }
+    if (c < 128) {
+        ++c;
+    }
+    return (int16_t)((c - 128) * SHRT_MAX / 127);
+}
+
+static uint64_t max1(uint64_t v) {
+    return v ? v : 1;
+}
+
+static int16_t eval_sawtooth(const s_haptic_waveform * wf, uint64_t loops, int up) {
+
+    int l1 = wf->l1, l2 = wf->l2, l0 = wf->l0;
+    if (l1 < l2) {
+        int tmp = l1; l1 = l2; l2 = tmp;
+    }
+    if (l0 > l1) {
+        l0 = l1;
+    } else if (l0 < l2) {
+        l0 = l2;
+    }
+    uint64_t span = (uint64_t)(l1 - l2) + 1;
+    uint64_t steps = loops / max1(wf->t3);
+    uint64_t advance = (steps * wf->step) % span;
+    int level;
+    if (up) {
+        level = l2 + (int)(((uint64_t)(l0 - l2) + advance) % span);
+    } else {
+        level = l1 - (int)(((uint64_t)(l1 - l0) + advance) % span);
+    }
+    return level_to_s16(level);
+}
+
+static int16_t eval_trapezoid(const s_haptic_waveform * wf, uint64_t loops) {
+
+    int l1 = wf->l1, l2 = wf->l2;
+    int amplitude = l1 > l2 ? l1 - l2 : l2 - l1;
+    uint64_t ramp = 0;
+    if (wf->step) {
+        // number of steps to go from one level to the other
+        ramp = ((uint64_t)amplitude + wf->step - 1) / wf->step;
+        ramp *= max1(wf->t3);
+    }
+    uint64_t cycle = (uint64_t)wf->t1 + ramp + wf->t2 + ramp;
+    if (cycle == 0) {
+        return level_to_s16(l1);
+    }
+    uint64_t p = loops % cycle;
+    if (p < wf->t1) {
+        return level_to_s16(l1);
+    }
+    p -= wf->t1;
+    if (p < ramp) {
+        int moved = (int)((p / max1(wf->t3)) * wf->step);
+        if (moved > amplitude) {
+            moved = amplitude;
+        }
+        return level_to_s16(l1 > l2 ? l1 - moved : l1 + moved);
+    }
+    p -= ramp;
+    if (p < wf->t2) {
+        return level_to_s16(l2);
+    }
+    p -= wf->t2;
+    int moved = (int)((p / max1(wf->t3)) * wf->step);
+    if (moved > amplitude) {
+        moved = amplitude;
+    }
+    return level_to_s16(l1 > l2 ? l2 + moved : l2 - moved);
+}
+
+static int16_t eval_rectangle(const s_haptic_waveform * wf, uint64_t loops) {
+
+    uint64_t period = (uint64_t)wf->t1 + wf->t2;
+    if (period == 0) {
+        return level_to_s16(wf->l1);
+    }
+    // hypothesis to confirm on hardware: the phase is an offset of 2*P loops in the period, ignored if P > T1 + T2
+    uint64_t offset = (wf->phase > period) ? 0 : (uint64_t)wf->phase * 2;
+    uint64_t p = (loops + offset) % period;
+    return level_to_s16(p < wf->t1 ? wf->l1 : wf->l2);
+}
+
+static int16_t eval_square(const s_haptic_waveform * wf, uint64_t loops, int * finished) {
+
+    uint64_t half = max1(wf->half_period);
+    uint64_t periods = wf->periods ? wf->periods : 256;
+    if (loops / (2 * half) >= periods) {
+        *finished = 1;
+        return 0;
+    }
+    int amplitude = (int)wf->amplitude * SHRT_MAX / UCHAR_MAX;
+    // hypothesis to confirm on hardware: the high state comes first
+    return ((loops % (2 * half)) < half) ? amplitude : -amplitude;
+}
+
+static int16_t eval_ramp(const s_haptic_waveform * wf, uint64_t loops, int * finished) {
+
+    int l1 = wf->l1, l2 = wf->l2;
+    int amplitude = l1 > l2 ? l1 - l2 : l2 - l1;
+    int hi = l1 > l2 ? l1 : l2;
+    int lo = l1 > l2 ? l2 : l1;
+    uint64_t moved = (loops / max1(wf->t3)) * wf->step;
+    if (wf->step == 0 || moved >= (uint64_t)amplitude) {
+        // the force stops once the target level is reached
+        if (wf->step != 0) {
+            *finished = 1;
+            return 0;
+        }
+        return level_to_s16(wf->direction ? hi : lo);
+    }
+    // D = 0: ramp up from L2 to L1, D = 1: reverse
+    return level_to_s16(wf->direction ? hi - (int)moved : lo + (int)moved);
+}
+
+static int16_t eval_variable(const s_haptic_waveform * wf, uint64_t loops) {
+
+    // the level is clipped to [0, 255] and remains there until the force is updated
+    int64_t moved = (int64_t)(loops / max1(wf->t3)) * wf->step;
+    int64_t level = wf->l1 + (wf->direction ? -moved : moved);
+    if (level < 0) {
+        level = 0;
+    } else if (level > UCHAR_MAX) {
+        level = UCHAR_MAX;
+    }
+    return level_to_s16((int)level);
+}
+
+int16_t haptic_waveform_eval(const s_haptic_waveform * wf, uint64_t elapsed_ns, int * finished) {
+
+    int dummy = 0;
+    if (finished == NULL) {
+        finished = &dummy;
+    }
+    *finished = 0;
+
+    uint64_t loops = elapsed_ns / HAPTIC_WAVEFORM_LOOP_NS;
+
+    switch (wf->kind) {
+    case E_WAVEFORM_SAWTOOTH_UP:
+        return eval_sawtooth(wf, loops, 1);
+    case E_WAVEFORM_SAWTOOTH_DOWN:
+        return eval_sawtooth(wf, loops, 0);
+    case E_WAVEFORM_TRAPEZOID:
+        return eval_trapezoid(wf, loops);
+    case E_WAVEFORM_RECTANGLE:
+        return eval_rectangle(wf, loops);
+    case E_WAVEFORM_SQUARE:
+        return eval_square(wf, loops, finished);
+    case E_WAVEFORM_RAMP:
+        return eval_ramp(wf, loops, finished);
+    case E_WAVEFORM_VARIABLE:
+        return eval_variable(wf, loops);
+    case E_WAVEFORM_NONE:
+    default:
+        break;
+    }
+    return 0;
+}
diff --git a/core/haptic/haptic_waveform.h b/core/haptic/haptic_waveform.h
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..0feb049
--- /dev/null
+++ b/core/haptic/haptic_waveform.h
@@ -0,0 +1,70 @@
+/*
+ License: GPLv3
+ */
+
+/*
+ * Time based Logitech "classic" effects (sawtooth, trapezoid, rectangle, square, ramp, variable).
+ *
+ * The evaluator is a pure function of the elapsed time since the effect was started.
+ * It has no dependency on the OS, on the sink or on the clock: the caller provides the elapsed time.
+ * This makes the result independent from the polling jitter and easy to unit test.
+ *
+ * Note: the waveform is sampled when the haptic core runs, i.e. once per controller refresh period (5 ms by default
+ * for the PS4 G29) and after each FFB report. Waveforms with a period shorter than two refresh periods are aliased.
+ *
+ * Reference: Logitech Force Feedback Protocol V1.6, chapter 3.2 (doc/Logitech_Force_Feedback_Protocol_V1.6.pdf).
+ */
+
+#ifndef HAPTIC_WAVEFORM_H_
+#define HAPTIC_WAVEFORM_H_
+
+#include <stdint.h>
+
+/*
+ * Duration of a Logitech "main loop", in nanoseconds.
+ * The specification gives 2 ms when the fixed time loop mode is on (CMD 0x0d).
+ * The duration is unspecified otherwise: 2 ms is assumed in both cases.
+ */
+#define HAPTIC_WAVEFORM_LOOP_NS 2000000ULL
+
+typedef enum {
+    E_WAVEFORM_NONE,
+    E_WAVEFORM_SAWTOOTH_UP,
+    E_WAVEFORM_SAWTOOTH_DOWN,
+    E_WAVEFORM_TRAPEZOID,
+    E_WAVEFORM_RECTANGLE,
+    E_WAVEFORM_SQUARE,
+    E_WAVEFORM_RAMP,
+    E_WAVEFORM_VARIABLE,
+} e_haptic_waveform_kind;
+
+/*
+ * Raw Logitech parameters. Levels are unsigned 8-bit values, 128 is the zero force.
+ */
+typedef struct {
+    e_haptic_waveform_kind kind;
+    uint8_t l0;        // initial level (sawtooth)
+    uint8_t l1;        // max level, or start level (variable)
+    uint8_t l2;        // min level
+    uint8_t t1;        // trapezoid/rectangle: duration of L1 (loops)
+    uint8_t t2;        // trapezoid/rectangle: duration of L2 (loops)
+    uint8_t t3;        // loops per step (sawtooth, trapezoid, ramp, variable)
+    uint8_t step;      // step size (sawtooth INC, trapezoid S, ramp S, variable S)
+    uint8_t phase;     // rectangle phase P
+    uint8_t direction; // ramp D, variable D (1 = decreasing)
+    uint8_t amplitude; // square A
+    uint16_t half_period; // square T (loops per state)
+    uint8_t periods;   // square N (0 means 256)
+} s_haptic_waveform;
+
+/*
+ * Evaluate the waveform.
+ *
+ * elapsed_ns: time since the effect started playing.
+ * finished: set to 1 if the effect ended by itself (ramp target reached, square periods played), 0 otherwise.
+ *
+ * Returns the force level in [-32767, 32767]. 0 is returned when the effect is finished.
+ */
+int16_t haptic_waveform_eval(const s_haptic_waveform * wf, uint64_t elapsed_ns, int * finished);
+
+#endif /* HAPTIC_WAVEFORM_H_ */
