+/*
+ License: GPLv3
+ */
+
+/*
+ * Computation level prediction of how many square wave transitions survive a given polling period.
+ * It runs the real haptic_synth with simulated ticks and a mock sink. It does NOT measure SDL nor the OS scheduler:
+ * the real restitution rate has to be measured with make HAPTIC_STATS=1 on the target system.
+ *
+ * usage: haptic_rate_sim [jitter_percent]   (the tick period is varied randomly by +/- jitter_percent)
+ */
+
+#include <stdio.h>
+#include <stdlib.h>
+
+#include <haptic/common/ff_lg.h>
+#include <haptic/haptic_synth.h>
+#include <gimx.h>
+
+s_gimx_params gimx_params = { 0 };
+
+#define LOOP HAPTIC_WAVEFORM_LOOP_NS
+#define MAX_DURATION_NS 4000000000ULL
+
+static void run(uint64_t tick_ns, unsigned int half_loops, int jitter, unsigned long * sent, unsigned long * expected, unsigned long * wrong) {
+
+    s_haptic_synth synth;
+    s_haptic_core_tweaks tweaks = { .invert = 0, .gain = { 100, 100, 100, 100 } };
+    s_haptic_core_data in, out;
+    unsigned char sq[7] = { 0x11, FF_LG_FTYPE_SQUARE_WAVE, 255, (unsigned char)half_loops, 0, 0, 0 };
+    uint64_t t0 = 1000000000ULL, t = t0;
+    int last = 0, valid = 0;
+    long expected_state;
+
+    // N = 0 means 256 periods: the effect ends by itself, stay clear of its end (one loop margin)
+    uint64_t duration = 256ULL * 2 * half_loops * LOOP - LOOP;
+    if (duration > MAX_DURATION_NS) {
+        duration = MAX_DURATION_NS;
+    }
+
+    haptic_synth_init(&synth);
+    ff_lg_convert_force(0, 0, (const s_ff_lg_command *)(sq + 1), 1, &in);
+    haptic_synth_feed(&synth, &in, t0);
+
+    *sent = *wrong = 0;
+    while (t - t0 < duration) {
+        if (haptic_synth_step(&synth, t, &tweaks, &out)) {
+            ++*sent;
+            // the level must follow the wave: check it against the state at the time of the tick
+            expected_state = (((t - t0) / LOOP) % (2 * half_loops)) < half_loops ? 1 : -1;
+            if ((out.constant.level > 0 ? 1 : -1) != expected_state) {
+                ++*wrong;
+            }
+            valid = 1;
+            last = out.constant.level;
+        }
+        uint64_t step = tick_ns;
+        if (jitter) {
+            long delta = (long)(((double)rand() / RAND_MAX * 2.0 - 1.0) * jitter / 100.0 * (double)tick_ns);
+            step = (uint64_t)((long)tick_ns + delta);
+        }
+        t += step;
+    }
+    (void)valid; (void)last;
+    // expected transitions: one per half period of the wave over the simulated duration
+    *expected = (unsigned long)(duration / (half_loops * LOOP));
+}
+
+int main(int argc, char ** argv) {
+
+    int jitter = argc > 1 ? atoi(argv[1]) : 0;
+    const uint64_t ticks_ms[] = { 5, 2, 1 };
+    const unsigned int halves[] = { 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 10, 16 };
+    unsigned int i, j;
+
+    printf("square wave transitions delivered to the sink (simulation, jitter +/-%d%%, 4 s per case)\n", jitter);
+    printf("%-8s %-12s %-10s %-9s %-9s %-10s %-11s\n", "tick", "half period", "wave freq", "expected", "sent", "delivered", "wrong level");
+    for (i = 0; i < sizeof(ticks_ms) / sizeof(*ticks_ms); ++i) {
+        for (j = 0; j < sizeof(halves) / sizeof(*halves); ++j) {
+            unsigned long sent, expected, wrong;
+            srand(1);
+            run(ticks_ms[i] * 1000000ULL, halves[j], jitter, &sent, &expected, &wrong);
+            printf("%2llu ms    %2u ms        %6.1f Hz  %-9lu %-9lu %5.1f %%     %-11lu\n", (unsigned long long)ticks_ms[i],
+                    halves[j] * 2, 1000.0 / (halves[j] * 4), expected, sent, expected ? 100.0 * sent / expected : 0.0, wrong);
+        }
+    }
+    return 0;
+}

+/*
+ License: GPLv3
+ */
+
+#include <stdio.h>
+#include <stdlib.h>
+#include <string.h>
+#include <haptic/haptic_stats.h>
+#include <gimx.h>
+
+s_gimx_params gimx_params = { 0 };
+
+static int failures = 0;
+
+#define CHECK(NAME, COND) do { \
+    int ok_ = (COND); \
+    printf("test case: %s: %s\n", NAME, ok_ ? "success" : "failure"); \
+    if (!ok_) { ++failures; } \
+} while (0)
+
+#define MS 1000000ULL
+
+int main(void) {
+
+    haptic_stats_reset();
+
+    // 1000 events every 5 ms, with one 12 ms gap and one 2.4 ms interval
+    uint64_t t = 1000 * MS;
+    int i;
+    for (i = 0; i < 1000; ++i) {
+        haptic_stats_event(HSTAT_CORE_UPDATE, t);
+        t += (i == 400) ? 12 * MS : (i == 700) ? 2400000ULL : 5 * MS;
+    }
+    const s_haptic_stat_series * s = haptic_stats.points + HSTAT_CORE_UPDATE;
+    CHECK("stats: n events gives n-1 intervals", s->count == 999);
+    CHECK("stats: min and max", s->min_ns == 2400000ULL && s->max_ns == 12 * MS);
+    CHECK("stats: threshold counters", s->gt1ms == 999 && s->gt2ms == 999 && s->gt5ms == 1 && s->gt10ms == 1);
+    uint64_t mean = haptic_stats_mean_ns(s);
+    CHECK("stats: mean close to 5 ms", mean > 4990000ULL && mean < 5010000ULL);
+    CHECK("stats: median is the 5 ms bucket", haptic_stats_percentile_ns(s, 50) >= 5 * MS && haptic_stats_percentile_ns(s, 50) <= 5100000ULL);
+    CHECK("stats: p99 not polluted by a single outlier", haptic_stats_percentile_ns(s, 99) <= 5100000ULL);
+    CHECK("stats: p100-like rank reaches the outlier", haptic_stats_percentile_ns(s, 100) >= 12 * MS);
+
+    // intervals above the histogram: many moderate delays (60-90 ms) and a single extreme one (3 s)
+    haptic_stats_reset();
+    uint64_t u = 1 * MS;
+    haptic_stats_event(HSTAT_SINK_UPDATE, u);
+    for (i = 0; i < 90; ++i) { // 90 intervals of 5 ms
+        u += 5 * MS;
+        haptic_stats_event(HSTAT_SINK_UPDATE, u);
+    }
+    for (i = 0; i < 9; ++i) { // 9 intervals of 60..92 ms
+        u += (60 + 4 * (uint64_t)i) * MS;
+        haptic_stats_event(HSTAT_SINK_UPDATE, u);
+    }
+    u += 3000 * MS; // 1 extreme interval
+    haptic_stats_event(HSTAT_SINK_UPDATE, u);
+    const s_haptic_stat_series * o = haptic_stats.points + HSTAT_SINK_UPDATE;
+    CHECK("overflow: 100 intervals, 10 above the histogram", o->count == 100 && o->overflow == 10);
+    CHECK("overflow: p95 is not the maximum", haptic_stats_percentile_ns(o, 95) < 200 * MS);
+    CHECK("overflow: p95 reflects the moderate delays", haptic_stats_percentile_ns(o, 95) >= 60 * MS);
+    CHECK("overflow: p99 and p100 reach the extreme delay", haptic_stats_percentile_ns(o, 100) == 3000 * MS);
+    CHECK("overflow: p99 stays in the moderate delays", haptic_stats_percentile_ns(o, 99) < 3000 * MS);
+    CHECK("overflow: bucket upper bound never exceeds the maximum", haptic_stats_percentile_ns(o, 91) <= o->max_ns);
+    haptic_stats_reset();
+    haptic_stats_event(HSTAT_SINK_UPDATE, 1 * MS);
+    haptic_stats_event(HSTAT_SINK_UPDATE, 200 * MS);
+    CHECK("overflow: a lone interval above the histogram", haptic_stats_percentile_ns(haptic_stats.points + HSTAT_SINK_UPDATE, 99) == 199 * MS);
+
+    // levels: requested by the synthesizer is tracked on its own
+    haptic_stats_reset();
+    haptic_stats_level(HSTAT_SYNTH_RESULT, 1 * MS, 100);
+    haptic_stats_level(HSTAT_SYNTH_RESULT, 2 * MS, 100);
+    haptic_stats_level(HSTAT_SYNTH_RESULT, 3 * MS, -100);
+    CHECK("levels: requested changes and repeats", haptic_stats.requested_changes == 1 && haptic_stats.requested_unchanged == 1);
+    CHECK("levels: nothing attempted yet", haptic_stats.attempted_n == 0 && haptic_stats.accepted_n == 0);
+
+    // ring keeps the most recent transitions
+    for (i = 0; i < 1000; ++i) {
+        haptic_stats_sdl_call((uint64_t)(10 + i) * MS, (uint64_t)(10 + i) * MS + 100000ULL, 0, 1, i);
+    }
+    CHECK("ring: total counter keeps counting", haptic_stats.attempted_n == 1000 && haptic_stats.accepted_n == 1000);
+    CHECK("ring: oldest entries are overwritten, latest kept",
+            haptic_stats.attempted[(haptic_stats.attempted_n - 1) % HSTAT_RING].level == 999);
+
+    // SDL calls: attempted vs accepted
+    haptic_stats_reset();
+    haptic_stats_sdl_call(10 * MS, 10 * MS + 300000ULL, 0, 1, 100);   // accepted constant
+    haptic_stats_sdl_call(15 * MS, 15 * MS + 900000ULL, -1, 1, -100); // refused constant
+    haptic_stats_sdl_call(20 * MS, 20 * MS + 200000ULL, 0, 0, 0);     // accepted spring
+    CHECK("sdl: durations recorded", haptic_stats.sdl_duration.count == 3 && haptic_stats.sdl_duration.max_ns == 900000ULL);
+    CHECK("sdl: errors and non constant counted", haptic_stats.sdl_errors == 1 && haptic_stats.sdl_non_constant == 1);
+    CHECK("sdl: a refused call is attempted but not accepted", haptic_stats.attempted_n == 2 && haptic_stats.accepted_n == 1
+            && haptic_stats.attempted_last == -100 && haptic_stats.accepted_last == 100);
+    CHECK("sdl: interval between attempted calls", haptic_stats.points[HSTAT_SDL_CALL].count == 2
+            && haptic_stats.points[HSTAT_SDL_CALL].min_ns == 5 * MS);
+    CHECK("sdl: accepted series skips the refused call", haptic_stats.points[HSTAT_SDL_ACCEPTED].count == 1
+            && haptic_stats.points[HSTAT_SDL_ACCEPTED].min_ns == 10 * MS);
+
+    // transition time stamps: ms modulo 1000 s, same time base for every series
+    haptic_stats_reset();
+    haptic_stats_level(HSTAT_SYNTH_RESULT, 1234567890123ULL, 42);
+    haptic_stats_sdl_call(1234567890123ULL, 1234567890123ULL + 1000, 0, 1, 42);
+    FILE * g = tmpfile();
+    haptic_stats_dump(g, 1);
+    rewind(g);
+    char text[8192] = { 0 };
+    size_t n = fread(text, 1, sizeof(text) - 1, g);
+    text[n] = '\0';
+    fclose(g);
+    CHECK("dump: time stamp is in ms modulo 1000 s", strstr(text, "[234567.890 ms:42]") != NULL);
+    CHECK("dump: requested, attempted and accepted series are printed", strstr(text, "requested transitions") && strstr(text, "attempted transitions")
+            && strstr(text, "accepted transitions"));
+
+    // periodic report
+    haptic_stats_reset();
+    FILE * f = tmpfile();
+    haptic_stats_report_if_due(f, 1000 * MS, 5000 * MS);
+    haptic_stats_report_if_due(f, 3000 * MS, 5000 * MS);
+    long before = ftell(f);
+    haptic_stats_report_if_due(f, 7000 * MS, 5000 * MS);
+    CHECK("report: nothing before the period, a summary after", before == 0 && ftell(f) > 0);
+    fclose(f);
+
+    printf("%d failure(s)\n", failures);
+    return failures ? EXIT_FAILURE : EXIT_SUCCESS;
+}

+/*
+ License: GPLv3
+ */
+
+#include <stdio.h>
+#include <string.h>
+#include <stdlib.h>
+
+#include <haptic/common/ff_lg.h>
+#include <haptic/haptic_waveform.h>
+#include <haptic/haptic_synth.h>
+#include <haptic/haptic_source.h>
+#include <gimx.h>
+
+s_gimx_params gimx_params = { 0 };
+
+#define LOOP HAPTIC_WAVEFORM_LOOP_NS
+
+static int failures = 0;
+
+#define CHECK(NAME, COND) do { \
+    int ok_ = (COND); \
+    printf("test case: %s: %s\n", NAME, ok_ ? "success" : "failure"); \
+    if (!ok_) { ++failures; } \
+} while (0)
+
+// decode a Logitech report (7 bytes: cmd, type, 5 parameters) for slot 0 and return the converted data
+static int decode(const unsigned char report[7], uint8_t slot, int playing, s_haptic_core_data * data) {
+    return ff_lg_convert_force(0, slot, (const s_ff_lg_command *)(report + 1), playing, data);
+}
+
+// check that every level of a waveform stays within [min, max] over many loops
+static int stays_within(const s_haptic_waveform * wf, int16_t min, int16_t max, uint64_t loops) {
+    uint64_t t;
+    for (t = 0; t < loops; ++t) {
+        int f;
+        int16_t v = haptic_waveform_eval(wf, t * LOOP, &f);
+        if (v < min || v > max) {
+            return 0;
+        }
+    }
+    return 1;
+}
+
+static void test_sawtooth(void) {
+    // L1=200 L2=100 L0=150 T3=2 INC=5
+    unsigned char r[7] = { 0x11, FF_LG_FTYPE_SAWTOOTH_UP, 200, 100, 150, 0x00, 0x25 };
+    s_haptic_core_data d;
+    CHECK("sawtooth up: decoded", decode(r, 0, 1, &d) && d.waveform_valid && d.waveform.kind == E_WAVEFORM_SAWTOOTH_UP
+            && d.type == E_DATA_TYPE_LOGITECH_RAW);
+    int f;
+    CHECK("sawtooth up: initial level is L0", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 0, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(150));
+    CHECK("sawtooth up: level after one step", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 2 * LOOP, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(155));
+    CHECK("sawtooth up: no step before T3 loops", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 1 * LOOP, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(150));
+    // 10 steps of 5 = +50 -> 200 (L1). span = 101: the 11th step gives (50 + 55) % 101 = 4 -> 104 (wrap to L2 side)
+    CHECK("sawtooth up: reaches L1", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 20 * LOOP, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(200));
+    CHECK("sawtooth up: first wrap-around value", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 22 * LOOP, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(104));
+    CHECK("sawtooth up: stays within [L2, L1]", stays_within(&d.waveform, ff_lg_u8_to_s16(100), ff_lg_u8_to_s16(200), 5000));
+    unsigned char rd[7] = { 0x11, FF_LG_FTYPE_SAWTOOTH_DOWN, 200, 100, 150, 0x00, 0x25 };
+    decode(rd, 0, 1, &d);
+    CHECK("sawtooth down: level after one step", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 2 * LOOP, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(145));
+    CHECK("sawtooth down: reaches L2", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 20 * LOOP, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(100));
+    CHECK("sawtooth down: first wrap-around value", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 22 * LOOP, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(196));
+    CHECK("sawtooth down: stays within [L2, L1]", stays_within(&d.waveform, ff_lg_u8_to_s16(100), ff_lg_u8_to_s16(200), 5000));
+    unsigned char rz[7] = { 0x11, FF_LG_FTYPE_SAWTOOTH_UP, 200, 100, 150, 0x00, 0x20 };
+    decode(rz, 0, 1, &d);
+    CHECK("sawtooth: INC = 0 keeps L0", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 1000 * LOOP, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(150));
+}
+
+static void test_trapezoid(void) {
+    // L1=228 L2=28 T1=4 T2=6 T3=1 S=10 -> ramp = 20 loops
+    unsigned char r[7] = { 0x11, FF_LG_FTYPE_TRAPEZOID, 228, 28, 4, 6, 0x1A };
+    s_haptic_core_data d;
+    int f;
+    decode(r, 0, 1, &d);
+    CHECK("trapezoid: starts at L1", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 0, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(228));
+    CHECK("trapezoid: holds L1 for T1 loops", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 3 * LOOP, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(228));
+    CHECK("trapezoid: falls by S per step", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 5 * LOOP, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(218));
+    CHECK("trapezoid: holds L2", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, (4 + 20 + 1) * LOOP, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(28));
+    CHECK("trapezoid: rises back", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, (4 + 20 + 6 + 2) * LOOP, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(48));
+    CHECK("trapezoid: periodic", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, (4 + 20 + 6 + 20) * LOOP, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(228));
+}
+
+static void test_rectangle_square(void) {
+    unsigned char r[7] = { 0x11, FF_LG_FTYPE_RECTANGLE, 200, 50, 3, 5, 0 };
+    s_haptic_core_data d;
+    int f;
+    decode(r, 0, 1, &d);
+    CHECK("rectangle: L1 during T1", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 2 * LOOP, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(200));
+    CHECK("rectangle: L2 during T2", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 3 * LOOP, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(50));
+    CHECK("rectangle: periodic", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 8 * LOOP, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(200));
+    // phase P = 2: offset of 4 loops (hypothesis), the period is 8 loops: starts in the L2 part
+    unsigned char rp[7] = { 0x11, FF_LG_FTYPE_RECTANGLE, 200, 50, 3, 5, 2 };
+    decode(rp, 0, 1, &d);
+    CHECK("rectangle: phase offset (2*P loops)", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 0, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(50)
+            && haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 4 * LOOP, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(200));
+    unsigned char rq[7] = { 0x11, FF_LG_FTYPE_RECTANGLE, 200, 50, 3, 5, 9 };
+    decode(rq, 0, 1, &d);
+    CHECK("rectangle: phase > T1 + T2 is ignored", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 0, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(200));
+
+    // A=255, T=4 loops, N=2 periods
+    unsigned char sq[7] = { 0x11, FF_LG_FTYPE_SQUARE_WAVE, 255, 4, 0, 2, 0 };
+    decode(sq, 0, 1, &d);
+    CHECK("square: high state first", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 0, &f) == SHRT_MAX);
+    CHECK("square: low state", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 4 * LOOP, &f) == -SHRT_MAX);
+    CHECK("square: still playing during period 2", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 8 * LOOP, &f) == SHRT_MAX && !f);
+    haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 16 * LOOP, &f);
+    CHECK("square: finished after N periods", f == 1);
+    unsigned char sq16[7] = { 0x11, FF_LG_FTYPE_SQUARE_WAVE, 255, 0x00, 0x01, 1, 0 };
+    decode(sq16, 0, 1, &d);
+    CHECK("square: 16 bits half period, low byte first", d.waveform.half_period == 256);
+}
+
+static void test_ramp_variable(void) {
+    // L1=200 L2=100 D=0 T=2 S=5: up from L2 to L1
+    unsigned char r[7] = { 0x11, FF_LG_FTYPE_RAMP, 200, 100, 0, 0x25, 0 };
+    s_haptic_core_data d;
+    int f;
+    decode(r, 0, 1, &d);
+    CHECK("ramp: starts at L2 (D=0)", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 0, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(100));
+    CHECK("ramp: rises by S every T loops", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 4 * LOOP, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(110));
+    haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 100 * LOOP, &f);
+    CHECK("ramp: stops at the target", f == 1);
+    unsigned char rr[7] = { 0x11, FF_LG_FTYPE_RAMP, 200, 100, 1, 0x25, 0 };
+    decode(rr, 0, 1, &d);
+    CHECK("ramp: starts at L1 (D=1)", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 0, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(200));
+
+    // variable force 0: L1=130 T1=1 S1=2 D1=1 (decreasing)
+    unsigned char v[7] = { 0x11, FF_LG_FTYPE_VARIABLE, 130, 128, 0x12, 0x00, 0x01 };
+    decode(v, 0, 1, &d);
+    CHECK("variable: decoded as waveform", d.type == E_DATA_TYPE_LOGITECH_RAW && d.waveform.kind == E_WAVEFORM_VARIABLE);
+    CHECK("variable: decreases", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 3 * LOOP, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(124));
+    // variable force 2: L2=120 T2=2 S2=3 D2=0 (increasing); the byte layout differs from force 0
+    unsigned char v2[7] = { 0x14, FF_LG_FTYPE_VARIABLE, 128, 120, 0x00, 0x23, 0x00 };
+    decode(v2, 2, 1, &d);
+    CHECK("variable: slot 2 uses L2/T2/S2/D2", d.waveform.kind == E_WAVEFORM_VARIABLE && d.waveform.l1 == 120
+            && haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 4 * LOOP, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(126));
+    unsigned char v1[7] = { 0x12, FF_LG_FTYPE_VARIABLE, 130, 128, 0x12, 0x00, 0x01 };
+    CHECK("variable: slots 1 and 3 are not supported as waveforms", decode(v1, 1, 1, &d) && !d.waveform_valid);
+    decode(v, 0, 1, &d);
+    CHECK("variable: clipped at 0", haptic_waveform_eval(&d.waveform, 1000 * LOOP, &f) == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(0));
+}
+
+static void test_conditions(void) {
+    s_haptic_core_data d;
+    unsigned char ac[7] = { 0x11, FF_LG_FTYPE_AUTO_CENTER_SPRING, 4, 4, 0xFF, 0, 0 };
+    CHECK("auto-center: converted to a spring", decode(ac, 0, 1, &d) && d.type == E_DATA_TYPE_SPRING && d.playing
+            && d.spring.center == 0 && d.spring.coefficient.left > 0 && d.spring.coefficient.right > 0);
+    unsigned char fr[7] = { 0x11, FF_LG_FTYPE_FRICTION, 128, 64, 255, 0x10, 0 };
+    CHECK("friction: converted to a damper", decode(fr, 0, 1, &d) && d.type == E_DATA_TYPE_DAMPER && d.damper.coefficient.left > 0
+            && d.damper.coefficient.right < 0);
+    CHECK("friction: stop", decode(fr, 0, 0, &d) && d.type == E_DATA_TYPE_DAMPER && !d.playing);
+}
+
+static void test_synth(void) {
+    s_haptic_synth synth;
+    s_haptic_core_tweaks tweaks = { .invert = 0, .gain = { 100, 100, 100, 100 } };
+    s_haptic_core_data in, out;
+    uint64_t t = 1000000000ULL;
+
+    haptic_synth_init(&synth);
+
+    // a constant alone goes through unchanged
+    unsigned char c[7] = { 0x11, FF_LG_FTYPE_CONSTANT, 200, 128, 128, 128, 0 };
+    decode(c, 0, 1, &in);
+    CHECK("synth: lone constant is not consumed", haptic_synth_feed(&synth, &in, t) == 0);
+    CHECK("synth: nothing to send without waveform", haptic_synth_step(&synth, t, &tweaks, &out) == 0);
+
+    // a square wave on slot 1 is consumed and summed with the constant
+    unsigned char sq[7] = { 0x12, FF_LG_FTYPE_SQUARE_WAVE, 100, 5, 0, 0, 0 };
+    decode(sq, 1, 1, &in);
+    CHECK("synth: waveform consumed", haptic_synth_feed(&synth, &in, t) == 1);
+    CHECK("synth: sends a constant", haptic_synth_step(&synth, t, &tweaks, &out) == 1 && out.type == E_DATA_TYPE_CONSTANT);
+    int expected = ff_lg_u8_to_s16(200) + 100 * SHRT_MAX / UCHAR_MAX;
+    CHECK("synth: constant + waveform", out.constant.level == expected);
+    CHECK("synth: no resend if nothing changed", haptic_synth_step(&synth, t + LOOP, &tweaks, &out) == 0);
+    CHECK("synth: level follows the waveform", haptic_synth_step(&synth, t + 5 * LOOP, &tweaks, &out) == 1
+            && out.constant.level == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(200) - 100 * SHRT_MAX / UCHAR_MAX);
+
+    // a constant update while a waveform plays is merged
+    unsigned char c2[7] = { 0x11, FF_LG_FTYPE_CONSTANT, 128, 128, 128, 128, 0 };
+    decode(c2, 0, 1, &in);
+    CHECK("synth: constant consumed while waveform plays", haptic_synth_feed(&synth, &in, t + 6 * LOOP) == 1);
+
+    // stop
+    decode(sq, 1, 0, &in);
+    CHECK("synth: stop consumed", haptic_synth_feed(&synth, &in, t + 7 * LOOP) == 1);
+    CHECK("synth: final constant sent", haptic_synth_step(&synth, t + 7 * LOOP, &tweaks, &out) == 1 && out.constant.level == 0);
+    CHECK("synth: back to idle", haptic_synth_step(&synth, t + 8 * LOOP, &tweaks, &out) == 0);
+
+    // gain and invert tweaks apply to the waveform
+    haptic_synth_init(&synth);
+    tweaks.invert = 1;
+    tweaks.gain.constant = 50;
+    decode(sq, 1, 1, &in);
+    haptic_synth_feed(&synth, &in, t);
+    haptic_synth_step(&synth, t, &tweaks, &out);
+    CHECK("synth: gain and invert applied", out.constant.level == -((100 * SHRT_MAX / UCHAR_MAX) * 50 / 100));
+}
+
+
+static int send_step(s_haptic_synth * synth, uint64_t now, const s_haptic_core_tweaks * tweaks, int * level) {
+    s_haptic_core_data out;
+    int ret = haptic_synth_step(synth, now, tweaks, &out);
+    if (ret) {
+        *level = out.constant.level;
+    }
+    return ret;
+}
+
+static void test_synth_mix(void) {
+    s_haptic_synth synth;
+    s_haptic_core_tweaks tweaks = { .invert = 0, .gain = { 100, 100, 100, 100 } };
+    s_haptic_core_data in;
+    uint64_t t = 5000000000ULL;
+    int level = 0;
+
+    // two square waves with different amplitudes and start times, and a constant force on slot 2
+    unsigned char a[7] = { 0x11, FF_LG_FTYPE_SQUARE_WAVE, 255, 4, 0, 0, 0 };
+    unsigned char b[7] = { 0x12, FF_LG_FTYPE_SQUARE_WAVE, 128, 4, 0, 0, 0 };
+    unsigned char c[7] = { 0x14, FF_LG_FTYPE_CONSTANT, 128, 128, 255, 128, 0 };
+    int b_amp = 128 * SHRT_MAX / UCHAR_MAX;
+
+    haptic_synth_init(&synth);
+    decode(a, 0, 1, &in);
+    haptic_synth_feed(&synth, &in, t);
+    CHECK("mix: first wave alone", send_step(&synth, t, &tweaks, &level) && level == SHRT_MAX);
+
+    decode(b, 1, 1, &in);
+    haptic_synth_feed(&synth, &in, t + 4 * LOOP);
+    CHECK("mix: second wave starts later, own phase", send_step(&synth, t + 4 * LOOP, &tweaks, &level)
+            && level == -SHRT_MAX + b_amp);
+    CHECK("mix: both waves, next half period", send_step(&synth, t + 8 * LOOP, &tweaks, &level)
+            && level == SHRT_MAX - b_amp);
+
+    decode(c, 2, 1, &in);
+    CHECK("mix: constant on slot 2 merged", haptic_synth_feed(&synth, &in, t + 9 * LOOP) == 1);
+    CHECK("mix: positive saturation", send_step(&synth, t + 9 * LOOP, &tweaks, &level) && level == SHRT_MAX);
+    // at t + 12 loops: wave A is low, wave B is high again, the constant is +SHRT_MAX
+    CHECK("mix: two waves and a constant", send_step(&synth, t + 12 * LOOP, &tweaks, &level)
+            && level == -SHRT_MAX + b_amp + SHRT_MAX);
+
+    // negative saturation: a low wave plus a negative constant on slot 3
+    haptic_synth_init(&synth);
+    unsigned char n[7] = { 0x18, FF_LG_FTYPE_CONSTANT, 128, 128, 128, 0, 0 };
+    decode(a, 0, 1, &in);
+    haptic_synth_feed(&synth, &in, t);
+    decode(n, 3, 1, &in);
+    haptic_synth_feed(&synth, &in, t);
+    CHECK("mix: negative saturation", send_step(&synth, t + 4 * LOOP, &tweaks, &level) && level == -SHRT_MAX);
+}
+
+static s_haptic_core_ids g27_ids = { USB_VENDOR_ID_LOGITECH, USB_PRODUCT_ID_LOGITECH_G27_WHEEL };
+
+// feed the synthesizer like haptic_core_update() does, return the number of waveform data received
+static int drain(const s_haptic_source * src, struct haptic_source_state * state, s_haptic_synth * synth, uint64_t now) {
+    s_haptic_core_data data;
+    int waveforms = 0;
+    while (src->get(state, &data)) {
+        if (data.waveform_valid) {
+            ++waveforms;
+        }
+        haptic_synth_feed(synth, &data, now);
+    }
+    return waveforms;
+}
+
+static void test_lifecycle(void) {
+    const s_haptic_source * src = haptic_source_get(g27_ids);
+    CHECK("lifecycle: Logitech source registered", src != NULL && src->ended != NULL);
+    if (src == NULL) {
+        return;
+    }
+    struct haptic_source_state * state = src->init(g27_ids);
+    s_haptic_synth synth;
+    s_haptic_core_tweaks tweaks = { .invert = 0, .gain = { 100, 100, 100, 100 } };
+    uint64_t t = 9000000000ULL;
+    int level = 0;
+    haptic_synth_init(&synth);
+
+    // ramp on slot 1: L1=200, L2=100, D=0, T=1, S=15 -> ends after 7 loops
+    unsigned char ramp[7] = { 0x11, FF_LG_FTYPE_RAMP, 200, 100, 0, 0x1F, 0x00 };
+    drain(src, state, &synth, t); // initial extended commands
+    src->process(state, sizeof(ramp), ramp);
+    CHECK("lifecycle: ramp starts", drain(src, state, &synth, t) == 1 && send_step(&synth, t, &tweaks, &level));
+
+    CHECK("lifecycle: ramp plays", send_step(&synth, t + 4 * LOOP, &tweaks, &level));
+    send_step(&synth, t + 100 * LOOP, &tweaks, &level);
+    unsigned int ended = haptic_synth_take_ended(&synth);
+    CHECK("lifecycle: natural end reported for slot 0", ended == 1);
+    CHECK("lifecycle: nothing reported twice", haptic_synth_take_ended(&synth) == 0);
+
+    // without telling the source, the identical replay is ignored: this is why the ended hook exists
+    src->process(state, sizeof(ramp), ramp);
+    CHECK("lifecycle: replay ignored while the source thinks the slot plays", drain(src, state, &synth, t + 101 * LOOP) == 0);
+
+    src->ended(state, 0);
+    src->process(state, sizeof(ramp), ramp);
+    CHECK("lifecycle: identical replay restarts after the end", drain(src, state, &synth, t + 102 * LOOP) == 1);
+    CHECK("lifecycle: replayed ramp starts from its first level", send_step(&synth, t + 102 * LOOP, &tweaks, &level)
+            && level == ff_lg_u8_to_s16(100));
+
+    // PLAY alone also restarts a finished effect
+    send_step(&synth, t + 300 * LOOP, &tweaks, &level);
+    src->ended(state, haptic_synth_take_ended(&synth) == 1 ? 0 : 3);
+    unsigned char play[7] = { 0x12, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0 };
+    src->process(state, sizeof(play), play);
+    CHECK("lifecycle: PLAY restarts a finished effect", drain(src, state, &synth, t + 301 * LOOP) == 1);
+
+    // a finite square wave (N = 1, T = 2 loops) ends then restarts with the same parameters
+    haptic_synth_init(&synth);
+    unsigned char sq[7] = { 0x21, FF_LG_FTYPE_SQUARE_WAVE, 255, 2, 0, 1, 0 };
+    src->process(state, sizeof(sq), sq);
+    CHECK("lifecycle: square starts", drain(src, state, &synth, t) >= 1);
+    send_step(&synth, t, &tweaks, &level);
+    send_step(&synth, t + 10 * LOOP, &tweaks, &level);
+    CHECK("lifecycle: finite square ended", haptic_synth_take_ended(&synth) == 2);
+    src->ended(state, 1);
+    src->process(state, sizeof(sq), sq);
+    CHECK("lifecycle: finite square can be replayed", drain(src, state, &synth, t + 20 * LOOP) == 1);
+
+    src->clean(state);
+}
+
+int main(void) {
+
+    test_sawtooth();
+    test_trapezoid();
+    test_rectangle_square();
+    test_ramp_variable();
+    test_conditions();
+    test_synth();
+    test_synth_mix();
+    test_lifecycle();
+
+    printf("%d failure(s)\n", failures);
+    return failures ? EXIT_FAILURE : EXIT_SUCCESS;
+}

+# Force feedback: measuring the computation and restitution rates
+
+Goal: tell apart (1) how often `haptic_core_update()` recomputes the synthesized force and (2) how often the SDL sink really receives and applies a new force, before changing anything in the synthesizer.
+
+## Build
+
+```
+make HAPTIC_STATS=1        # in core/, adds -DHAPTIC_STATS
+```
+
+Without `HAPTIC_STATS` the `HSTAT_*` macros are empty, `core/haptic/haptic_stats.c` is left out of the core build (`core/Makefile`), and the core keeps no diagnostic field: there is no code, no data and no behaviour change. The unit tests build `haptic_stats.c` on their own. The statistics are not thread safe: they are a diagnostic, not a feature.
+
+## What is measured
+
+Nothing is logged per event. Intervals between events go into 100 us histograms; a summary is printed to stderr every 5 s and when the core is cleaned. Resolution of the median/p95/p99 values is 100 us (upper bound of the bucket) up to 51.2 ms; above, intervals go into power of two buckets (51.2-102.4 ms, 102.4-204.8 ms, ...) and a percentile is the upper bound of its bucket, never above the real maximum. Only a percentile falling in the last bucket (above 6.5 s) is reported as the maximum.
+
+| Line | Point |
+|---|---|
+| `haptic_core_update (all)` | every entry of `haptic_core_update()` (periodic refresh and after a report) |
+| `haptic_core_update (after report)` | entries that followed the reception of an FFB report (a report processed by `haptic_core_process_report()` since the previous update) |
+| `haptic_synth_step` | computations of the synthesized force |
+| `synth result requested` | computations whose result had to be sent to the sink |
+| `sink->process` | commands handed to the sink (any type: constant, spring, damper) |
+| `sink->update` | calls to `sink->update()` (a no-op for the OS sink) |
+| `SDL call attempted` | every call to `ginput_joystick_set_haptic()`, which is synchronous down to SDL |
+| `SDL call accepted` | the same calls, only those that returned a non negative value |
+| `SDL call duration` | duration of each call: the software submission time (processing and hand-over to the driver). It is not the delay between the request and the mechanical reaction of the wheel, which this instrumentation cannot see |
+
+Also printed: SDL errors (the return value was ignored before), SDL calls that were not a constant force, and the constant levels (changes and unchanged repeats) at three stages: requested by the synthesizer, attempted (passed to SDL, whatever the result) and accepted (SDL returned success). A refused call is attempted but never accepted. The most recent transitions of the three series are printed as `[time in ms modulo 1000 s : level]`, with the same time base, so that requested, attempted and accepted transitions can be compared.
+
+The SDL operation itself (`SDL_HapticNewEffect`, `SDL_HapticUpdateEffect`, `SDL_HapticRunEffect`) happens inside `sdlinput_joystick_set_haptic()` in the `shared/gimxinput` submodule: this instrumentation measures the whole call and cannot tell which SDL function dominates. Instrument the submodule only if the duration turns out to be the limiting factor.
+
+## Protocol
+
+Same duration (60 s) and same effect parameters for each trial. Run on Linux and Windows if possible, with `--refresh 5`, `--refresh 2` and `--refresh 1` (ms). Stop GIMX normally so that the final summary is printed.
+
+1. Idle: no effect (background rate of the periodic calls).
+2. Constant force: does SDL receive repeated updates while the level does not change? (`unchanged` counters)
+3. Slow square wave (half period 20 ms): does every transition give one SDL call?
+4. Fast square wave (half periods 2, 4, 6, 8 ms): from which period do transitions disappear?
+5. Ramp: regularity of the transmitted levels.
+6. Combined load: several simultaneous effects (two waves and a constant), to see the cost of the mix.
+
+## Reading the results
+
+| Observation | Likely limiting factor |
+|---|---|
+| `haptic_core_update` slow | upstream of the sink: scheduler or controller refresh |
+| core fast, `sink->process` slow | the synthesizer does not produce enough updates |
+| `sink->process` fast, SDL calls slow | the sink or the SDL update mechanism |
+| SDL regular but transitions missing | the sampling rate is too low for the waveform |
+| very variable intervals | scheduling jitter: look at p99 and the `>Nms` counters, not at the mean |
+
+## Decision grid
+
+| Observation | Decision to consider |
+|---|---|
+| SDL calls regular and fast, transitions missing | sampling limit of the synthesizer |
+| SDL calls spaced out or irregular | look at the sink and the scheduler |
+| SDL calls regular but long | instrument `SDL_HapticNewEffect`, `SDL_HapticUpdateEffect` and `SDL_HapticRunEffect` separately in `shared/gimxinput` |
+| SDL errors not zero | diagnose the failures first |
+| requested and accepted levels diverge | examine the sending conditions and the errors before any rate change |
+
+## Report to produce
+
+OS, device, refresh period; the summaries of the six trials; an excerpt of requested vs transmitted transitions; the limiting factor. Do not change the synthesizer rate before this report exists.
+
+## Computation level prediction (no hardware)
+
+`make -C core/test/haptic haptic_rate_sim && core/test/haptic/haptic_rate_sim [jitter_percent]` runs the real synthesizer with simulated ticks and a mock sink, for a square wave. It predicts what the polling period alone does, and says nothing about SDL or the scheduler. With no jitter, 100 % of the transitions of a square wave of half period >= 6 ms are delivered at a 5 ms tick, but only about 60 % at 4 ms (125 Hz) and about 20 % at 2 ms (250 Hz); at a 2 ms or 1 ms tick every transition is delivered. Compare the measured numbers with its output to separate the sampling limit from the SDL/OS limit.

+2 Introduction
+
+This document deﬁnes parts of the Logitech Force Feedback protocol. Some commands used for testing/debugging and
+other purposes have been omitted from this document. However, the given information should be suﬃcient to implement
+a fully functional open-source force feedback driver.
+
+2.1 Revision History
+
+ RBosa, July 2014: First release; V1.0
+ Rbosa, July 2014: Second release, incorporated clariﬁcations and feedback from Edwin Velds; added EXT_CMD 0x0a,
+
+       EXT_CMD 0x10, EXT_CMD 0x11 and EXT_CMD 0x12; V1.1
+ Rbosa, July 2014: Added appendix listing all known wheels and their bcdVersion in the various modes; added table to
+
+       list supported commands to switch identity; V1.2
+ Rbosa, July 2014: Corrected Edwin’s name; added G27 to Table 57; ﬁxed some spelling mistakes; clariﬁed spring
+
+       coeﬃcient swapping rule for older devices; added illustration of force position reporting vs. force location; V1.3
+ Rbosa, July/August 2015: Converted to PdfTex; added distinction between Classic Force Protocol and the new HID++
+
+       Force Protocol; ﬁxed many typos and consistency issues; V1.4
+ Rbosa, December 2015: Added new HID++ Force eﬀect types; V1.5
+ Dpursey, December 2020: Added new HID++ RPM Indicator feature; V1.6
+
+2.2 Scope
+
+There are two protocols described in this document.
+The ﬁrst protocol (“Classic”) described in this document has been used in almost all force feedback-enabled devices that
+Logitech has produced. It is used for joysticks and wheels. Each device may slightly deviate from the protocol by not
+implementing certain commands or using slight variations of a command.
+The second protocol (“HID++”) is used for force feedback on
+
+    • G920 Driving Force Racing Wheel for Xbox.
+    • G923 TRUEFORCE Racing Wheel for Xbox.
+HID++ is also used to control the RPM Leds on
+    • G923 TRUEFORCE Racing Wheel for Playstation.
+    • G923 TRUEFORCE Racing Wheel for Xbox.
+
+2.3 Capabilities
+
+2.3.1 Classic Force Feedback
+    • The protocol provides feedback for a device with one or two force-enabled axes.
+    • Internally, each force-axis position is encoded in 8 bits, even if that axis is reported in a diﬀerent size to the host.
+    • Each axis can be driven with two eﬀects and a default spring for joysticks. For wheels, four eﬀects and two default
+       springs can be combined.
+    • For joysticks, the two axes are completely independent. In order to play a force at a 45 degree angle, it is up to the
+       host to separate it into an X and Y component.
+Logitech Force Feedback Protocol V1.6  Page 5 of 44
+
+2.3.2 HID++ Force Feedback
+    • The protocol allows for multiple force-enabled axes. The axis type is identiﬁed with each eﬀect.
+    • There are many force eﬀect slots, each of which can be assigned to any axis.
+    • All parameters are given as 16-bit numbers (signed and unsigned, varying on the parameter). Internally, most forces
+       are calculated with float precision.
+
+2.3.3 HID++ RPM Leds
+    • The protocol details the RPM Leds capabilities of the device.
+    • There are two basic mode types ‘Direct‘ and ‘Pattern‘.
+Logitech Force Feedback Protocol V1.6                                                                 Page 6 of 44
+
+3 Classic Format
+
+Commands are sent to the device via HID Output Reports. These are sent from the host through either an Interrupt Out
+endpoint (usually EP1) or the default pipe (EP0). HID Output Reports sent over EP0 always use the ﬁrst byte for the
+Report ID. This means the available space for the command and its parameters is seven bytes. The seven bytes are deﬁned
+as vendor-speciﬁc data in the HID descriptor. The command payload has the following format:
+
+              Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0
+
+              0   F3                   F2  F1     F0                      CMD
+
+              1                            FORCE_TYPE or CMD_PARAM
+
+              2          Force/Command parameter 1 (or 0x00 if not used)
+
+              3          Force/Command parameter 2 (or 0x00 if not used)
+
+              4          Force/Command parameter 3 (or 0x00 if not used)
+
+              5          Force/Command parameter 4 (or 0x00 if not used)
+
+              6          Force/Command parameter 5 (or 0x00 if not used)
+
+                                           Table 1: Output Report
+
+Parameter         Value                Deﬁnition
+
+F0...F3               0/1              Each bit indicates to which force slot the command applies to
+CMD              see Table 3           One of the commands listed in section 3.1
+FORCE_TYPE       see Table 23          One of the force types listed in section 3.2
+CMD_PARAM        see Table 52          One of the command parameters listed in section 3.3
+
+                                       Table 2: Output Report Parameters
+
+3.1 Commands
+
+                  CMD                  Command
+
+                  0x00                 Download Force
+                  0x01                 Download and Play Force
+                  0x02                 Play Force
+                  0x03                 Stop Force
+                  0x04                 Default Spring On
+                  0x05                 Default Spring Oﬀ
+                  0x06                 [reserved, do not use]
+                  0x07                 [reserved, do not use]
+                  0x08                 Turn on Normal Mode (see CMD 0x0b)
+                  0x09                 Set LED
+                  0x0a                 Set Watchdog
+                  0x0b                 Turn on Raw Mode (see CMD 0x08)
+                  0x0c                 Refresh Force
+                  0x0d                 Fixed Time Loop
+                  0x0e                 Set Default Spring
+                  0x0f                 Set Dead Band
+                  0xf8                 Extended Command
+
+                                            Table 3: Commands
+Logitech Force Feedback Protocol V1.6                                                        Page 7 of 44
+
+3.1.1 CMD 0x00 – Download Force
+
+A force is downloaded from the host into the force slot(s), to be played at a later time. Selecting multiple forces (F0...F3)
+is allowed.
+
+Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0
+
+0      F3                              F2  F1  F0                         0x00
+
+1                                              FORCE_TYPE
+
+2...6                                  Force parameters (or 0x00 if not used)
+
+                                       Table 4: Download Force Report
+
+3.1.2 CMD 0x01 – Download and Play Force                                                     Selecting multiple forces
+A force is downloaded from the host into the force slot(s), and starts playing immediately.
+(F0...F3) is allowed.
+
+Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0
+
+0      F3                              F2  F1  F0                         0x01
+
+1                                              FORCE_TYPE
+
+2...6                                  Force parameters (or 0x00 if not used)
+
+                                 Table 5: Download Force and Play Report
+
+3.1.3 CMD 0x02 – Play Force
+Starts playing the selected force(s). Selecting multiple forces (F0...F3) is allowed. Assumes that the selected forces have
+been previously downloaded.
+
+Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0
+
+0      F3                              F2  F1  F0                         0x02
+
+1...6                                              0x00
+
+                                           Table 6: Play Force Report
+
+3.1.4 CMD 0x03 – Stop Force
+Stops playing the selected force(s). Selecting multiple forces (F0...F3) is allowed.
+
+Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1                                        Bit 0
+
+0      F3                              F2  F1  F0                         0x03
+
+1...6                                              0x00
+
+                                           Table 7: Stop Force Report
+
+3.1.5 CMD 0x04 – Default Spring On
+Turns on the default spring(s). Setting F0 and/or F1 selects spring X. Setting F2 and/or F3 selects spring Y. Selecting
+both springs is allowed.
+
+Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0
+
+0      F3                              F2  F1  F0                         0x04
+
+1...6                                              0x00
+
+                                       Table 8: Default Spring On Report
+Logitech Force Feedback Protocol V1.6                                                      Page 8 of 44
+
+3.1.6 CMD 0x05 – Default Spring Oﬀ
+Turns oﬀ the default spring(s). Setting F0 and/or F1 selects spring X. Setting F2 and/or F3 selects spring Y. Selecting
+both springs is allowed.
+
+Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0
+
+0      F3                              F2  F1  F0                         0x05
+
+1...6                                              0x00
+
+                                       Table 9: Default Spring Oﬀ Report
+
+3.1.7 CMD 0x08 – Normal Mode / Extended Command
+The F0...F3 bits have a special meaning for CMD 0x08. If all the F0...F3 bits are zero, this command will restore the normal
+content of the device input report after operating in Raw Mode (see chapter 3.1.10).
+
+Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0
+
+0      0                               0   0   0                          0x08
+
+1...6                                              0x00
+
+                                       Table 10: Restore Normal Mode Report
+
+If all the F0...F3 bits are one, this command is an extended command (see chapter 3.3).
+
+Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0
+
+0      1                               1   1   1                          0x08
+
+1                                              EXT_CMD
+
+2...6     Extended Command Parameters (or 0x00 if not used)
+
+                                       Table 11: Extended Command Report
+
+3.1.8 CMD 0x09 – Set LED
+Turns the device LEDs on (1) or oﬀ (0). The LEDs are allocated on a per-device basis.
+
+Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0
+
+0      0                               0   0   0                          0x09
+
+1 LED7 LED6 LED5 LED4 LED3 LED2 LED1 LED0
+
+2...6                                              0x00
+
+                                           Table 12: Set LED Report
+
+3.1.9 CMD 0x0a – Set Watchdog
+Turns on the watchdog and sets the watchdog duration (in number of main loop executions).
+
+Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0
+
+0      0                               0   0   0                          0x0a
+
+1                                              WATCHDOG
+
+2...6                                              0x00
+
+                                       Table 13: Set Watchdog Report
+Logitech Force Feedback Protocol V1.6                                                                  Page 9 of 44
+
+Parameter     Value                    Description
+WATCHDOG
+                0x00                   Watch dog is turned oﬀ.
+           0x01...0xff                 Device will go through a reset, if no Output Report is received during
+                                       the speciﬁed period (number of main loop executions).
+
+                        Table 14: Set Watchdog Report Parameters
+
+3.1.10 CMD 0x0b – Raw Mode
+Turns on Raw Mode for the device. Each device has its own report format deﬁnition in Raw Mode. The device will
+continue to send raw report data until it receives a CMD 0x08, or it gets power-cycled.
+
+Raw Mode is required to get the actual, uncalibrated axis values for exact position tracking for springs and to improve the
+calibration algorithms implemented in ﬁrmware. However, each device deﬁnes a unique form of raw data report. These
+reports will very likely not ﬁt into the ﬁelds described by the HID descriptor of the device. Therefore, the data sent from
+the device needs to be intercepted before it reaches HID and appropriately modiﬁed.
+
+           Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0
+
+           0         0                 0   0        0                        0x0b
+
+           1...6                                        0x00
+
+                                           Table 15: Raw Mode Report
+
+3.1.11 CMD 0x0c – Refresh Force
+Changes the parameters of a force that is already playing. Multiple forces (F0...F3) may be selected.
+
+After receiving this command, the force continues on from its most recent level. If this command is executed with a
+diﬀerent force type than the one that is already playing, the result is undeﬁned (and may cause undesired side-eﬀects).
+
+           Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0
+
+           0      F3                   F2  F1       F0                       0x0c
+
+           1                                        FORCE_TYPE
+
+           2...6                          Force parameters (or 0x00 if not used)
+
+                                       Table 16: Refresh Force Report
+
+3.1.12 CMD 0x0d – Fixed Time Loop
+Makes the main ﬁrmware loop run at a constant speed or as fast as possible.
+
+           Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1                          Bit 0
+
+           0         0                 0   0        0                        0x0d
+
+           1                                        FIXED_LOOP
+
+           2..6                                         0x00
+
+                                       Table 17: Fixed Time Loop Report
+Logitech Force Feedback Protocol V1.6                                                            Page 10 of 44
+
+Parameter   Value Description
+FIXED_LOOP
+            0x00                       Fixed loop mode oﬀ (main loop runs as fast as possible).
+            0x01                       Fixed loop mode on (main loop updates forces every 2ms).
+
+                                       Table 18: Fixed Loop Report Parameters
+
+3.1.13 CMD 0x0e – Set Default Spring
+Sets the parameters of the default spring. Setting F0 and/or F1 selects spring X. Setting F2 and/or F3 selects spring Y.
+Selecting both springs is allowed. The parameters are the same as for the auto-centering force type (see chapter 3.2.4).
+
+Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0
+
+0           F3                         F2  F1  F0                      0x0e
+
+1                                                  0x00
+
+2           0                          0   0   0         0                     K1
+
+3           0                          0   0   0         0                     K2
+
+4                                                  CLIP
+
+5..6                                               0x00
+
+                                       Table 19: Set Default Spring Report
+
+3.1.14 CMD 0x0f – Set Dead Band
+Controls the exterior dead band. The power-up default is on. If dead band is on, the axis values are clipped at the extremes
+of their travel and reported as min/max respectively.
+
+Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0
+
+0           0                          0   0   0                       0x0f
+
+1                                              DEAD_BAND
+
+2..6                                               0x00
+
+                                       Table 20: Set Dead Band Report
+
+                Parameter                      Value Description
+
+                 DEAD_BAND 0x00 Dead band is oﬀ.
+                                        0x01 Dead band is on.
+
+                Table 21: Set Dead Band Report Parameters
+Logitech Force Feedback Protocol V1.6                                                     Page 11 of 44

+3.2 Force Types and Parameters
+
+Wheels report the wheel position with 0 on the left side and 255 on the right side:
+
+Force levels are generally indicated with a single byte according to the encoding below:
+
+Level       Deﬁnition
+  0         Maximum force ’located’ towards the ’0’ end of the axis.
+            The wheel will spin clock-wise under the inﬂuence of such a force:
+
+127 or 128  No force
+    255     Maximum force ’located’ towards the ’255’ end of the axis.
+            The wheel will spin counter-clock-wise under the inﬂuence of such a force:
+
+            Table 22: Force Levels
+Logitech Force Feedback Protocol V1.6                                                   Page 12 of 44
+
+Classic Force Feedback implements the following force types:
+
+   FORCE_TYPE                          Force
+
+       0x00                            Constant
+       0x01                            Spring
+       0x02                            Damper
+       0x03                            Auto-Centering Spring
+       0x04                            Sawtooth Up
+       0x05                            Sawtooth Down
+       0x06                            Trapezoid
+       0x07                            Rectangle
+       0x08                            Variable
+       0x09                            Ramp
+       0x0a                            Square Wave (not implemented on all devices)
+       0x0b                            High-Resolution Spring
+       0x0c                            High-Resolution Damper
+       0x0d                            High-Resolution Auto-Centering Spring
+       0x0e                            Friction (not implemented on all devices)
+
+                                            Table 23: Force Types
+
+3.2.1 FORCE_TYPE 0x00 – Constant
+A constant force keeps pushing or pulling with the indicated level. It is independent of position or velocity of the axis.
+The direction is encoded as the sign of the force level (see tabel 22).
+
+Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0
+
+0  F3                                  F2  F1  F0                          CMD
+
+1                                                             0x00
+
+2                                              Force F0 level
+
+3                                              Force F1 level
+
+4                                              Force F2 level
+
+5                                              Force F3 level
+
+6                                                             0x00
+
+                                       Table 24: Constant Force Type Report
+
+3.2.2 FORCE_TYPE 0x01 – Spring
+A spring force creates an asymmetric force around a central dead-band area. The force is zero within the dead band. It
+rises linearly just outside the dead band with a slope given by the slope coeﬃcient and saturates at the clip value. Each
+side of the axis can be inverted individually.
+
+Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0
+
+0  F3                                  F2  F1  F0                          CMD
+
+1                                                             0x01
+
+2                                                             D1
+
+3                                                             D2
+
+4  0                                       K2                       0           K1
+
+5  0                                   0   0   S2                   0  0        0   S1
+
+6                                                             CLIP
+
+                                       Table 25: Spring Force Type Report
+Logitech Force Feedback Protocol V1.6                                                      Page 13 of 44
+
+                                       Figure 1: Spring Parameters
+
+Parameter  Value                                  Deﬁnition
+
+    D1         0...255                          Lower limit of central dead band
+    D2                                          Upper limit of central dead band
+    K1         0...255                 Low (left or push) side spring constant selector
+    K2     see Table 27/28             High (right or pull) side spring constant selector
+    S1     see Table 27/28                  Low side slope inversion (1 = inverted)
+    S2                                      High side slope inversion (1 = inverted)
+   CLIP           0/1                     Clip level (maximum force), on either side
+
+                  0/1
+
+               0...255
+
+                                       Table 26: Spring Force Parameters
+
+The spring constants K1 and K2 deﬁne the spring coeﬃcient (slope) according to Table 27. The oﬀset is deﬁned as the
+diﬀerence of the current position and the nearest dead band limit.
+
+                                       K Value Spring Coeﬃcient
+
+                                         0x00      1/4 of oﬀset
+                                         0x01      1/2 of oﬀset
+                                         0x02      3/4 of oﬀset
+                                         0x03      Force = oﬀset
+                                         0x04      3/2 of oﬀset
+                                         0x05      2 times oﬀset
+                                         0x06      3 times oﬀset
+                                         0x07      4 times oﬀset
+                                       Table 27:  Spring Coeﬃcients
+
+Note that for the following devices, the coeﬃcient table entries 5 and 6 are swapped and follow Table 28 instead:
+    • Logitech Driving Force (PID_C294)
+    • Logitech Driving Force EX (PID_C294)
+    • Logitech Racing Force EX (PID_C294)
+    • Logitech Driving Force RX (PID_C294)
+    • Logitech Formula Force GP (PID_C293)
+    • Logitech Force 3D (PID_C283)
+Logitech Force Feedback Protocol V1.6                                                     Page 14 of 44
+
+                                           K Value Spring Coeﬃcient
+
+                                             0x00      1/4 of oﬀset
+                                             0x01      1/2 of oﬀset
+                                             0x02      3/4 of oﬀset
+                                             0x03      Force = oﬀset
+                                             0x04      3/2 of oﬀset
+                                             0x05      3 times oﬀset
+                                             0x06      2 times oﬀset
+                                             0x07      4 times oﬀset
+                                           Table 28:  Spring Coeﬃcients
+
+3.2.3 FORCE_TYPE 0x02 – Damper
+Direction and speed are provided by an external diﬀerentiator circuit, or by subtracting the current position from the
+position recorded the last time the function ran. The speed is then multiplied by the K1/K2 parameters. The K1 and
+K2 force multipliers use the same scale encoding as a spring force (see previous section). K1 is applied when moving to
+the left/pushing, while K2 is applied when moving right/pulling. If the S1 or S2 parameter is 0, then the force generated
+opposes the movement (standard damper action). If it is 1, the force generated accentuates the movement (i.e., a negative
+damper or “ice” eﬀect).
+
+Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0
+
+0  F3                                  F2     F1      F0                    CMD
+
+1                                                         0x02
+
+2  0                                   0      0       0         0                K1
+
+3  0                                   0      0       0         0        0       0   S1
+
+4  0                                   0      0       0         0                K2
+
+5  0                                   0      0       0         0        0       0   S2
+
+6                                                         0x00
+
+                                       Table 29: Damper Force Type Report
+
+Parameter                              Value Deﬁnition
+
+K1                                     0...7  Low (left or push) side damper coeﬃcient
+K2                                     0...7  High (right or pull) side damper coeﬃcient
+S1                                      0/1   Low side inversion (1 = inverted)
+S2                                      0/1   High side inversion (1 = inverted)
+
+                                       Table 30: Damper Force Parameters
+Logitech Force Feedback Protocol V1.6                                                    Page 15 of 44
+
+3.2.4 FORCE_TYPE 0x03 – Auto-Centering Spring
+The center of the Auto-Centering Spring eﬀect is dynamically set as the center of the axis. The dead band area is 2 counts
+wide from (axis_center-1) to (axis_center+1) This force computes the center of the axis travel using the following
+formula: axis_center = (Max/2) + (Min/2). When the force is ﬁrst downloaded, Min is set to 255 and Max to 0. K1,
+K2 and CLIP are coded the same way as a regular spring (see chapter 3.2.2).
+
+Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0
+
+0      F3                              F2      F1        F0              CMD
+
+1                                                            0x03
+
+2      0                               0       0         0         0          K1
+
+3      0                               0       0         0         0          K2
+
+4                                                            CLIP
+
+5...6                                                        0x00
+
+       Table 31: Auto-Centering Spring Force Type Report
+

+3.2.7 FORCE_TYPE 0x06 – Trapezoid
+The frequency range of this periodic force depends somewhat on the amplitude because each step cannot be greater than
+15 counts. If the amplitude is reduced in half, the maximum frequency is almost doubled. The initial force level is L1.
+The rising and falling times are the same. If T1 = T2 = 0, then the wave is a triangle.
+
+Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0

+L2         0...255                         Minimum Force Level

+T2         0...255                         Duration (in main loops) of L2 Level
+T3         0...15                          Number of main loops between adding/subtracting S

+3.2.12 FORCE_TYPE 0x0b – High-Resolution Spring
+This force type allows more precise control of the spring constant than the FORCE_TYPE 0x01 Spring Eﬀect.
+Parameter deﬁnitions are identical to FORCE_TYPE 0x01 springs, but with an additional 8 selections for the spring constant
+(which is represented in 4 bits instead of 3). These coeﬃcients have been tuned for each device to be fairly linear. Hence
+a coeﬃcient should be scaled linearly from 0 to 15 for K1 and K2 according to the desired spring coeﬃcient.
+
+           Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0
+

+
+           6                                           CLIP
+

+
+Parameter  Value Deﬁnition
+D1, D2
+K1, K2     0...255     Deadband on the negative and positive side of the spring.
+           0...15      Linear slope with 0 being the weakest force and 15 being the strongest.
+S1, S2                 The actual coeﬃcient factor is device dependent.
+              0/1      Sign of the slope parameter.
+

+deadbands for a total of 11 bit precision. It is backward compatible with older implementations of this force in that the
+additional low order bits are located in otherwise-ignored bit ﬁelds.

+3.2.15 FORCE_TYPE 0x0e – Friction (only select devices)
+This force is used to generate a resistance in movement. It is similar to a damper eﬀect in that it generates a force against
+velocity. It is distinct from a damper in that it the force eﬀect decreases as the velocity increases.
+
+This force is only supported on the following devices:
+    • Logitech Driving Force Pro (PID_C298)
+    • Logitech G25 Racing Wheel (PID_C299)
+    • Logitech Driving Force GT (PID_C29A)
+    • Logitech G27 Racing Wheel (PID_C29B)
+
+Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0

+3.3 Extended Commands
+
+Extended Commands provide device-speciﬁc functionality. They are used to switch modes of newer devices, which start in
+a compatibility mode. They are also used to change the amount of wheel rotation of some wheel devices.
+

+         0x0a                          Revert Identity
+         0x10                          Switch to G25 Identity with USB Detach
+         0x11                          Switch to G25 Identity without USB Detach
+         0x12                          Set RPM LEDs
+         0x81                          Wheel Range Change
+                                       Table 52: Extended Commands
+

+4 HID++ Force Feedback
+
+The Logitech G920 wheel includes a native Xbox One Force Feedback mode and a PC mode. The PC mode is implemented
+as a HID++ Feature.
+
+A HID++ command has a 4-byte header. After the header are the payload bytes of the speciﬁc method. The following
+description only lists the payload content for the requests and the responses.
+
+For more information on the HID++ header and the HID++ speciﬁcation see HID++ Header.
+
+4.1 x8123 Force Feedback
+
+The HID++ Feature x8123 implements multi-axis force feedback control. The feature has the following methods and
+notiﬁcations:
+
+                                                            Index Name
+                                                               0 getInfo
+                                                               1 resetAll
+                                                               2 downloadEﬀect
+                                                               3 setEﬀectState
+                                                               4 destroyEﬀect
+                                                               5 getAperture
+                                                               6 setAperture
+                                                               7 getGlobalGains
+                                                               8 setGlobalGains
+                                                               0 forceHasFinished
+
+                                                Table 66: x8123 Methods and Notiﬁcations
+
+4.1.1 x8123.0 getInfo
+The getInfo method returns static data. Data that does not change for the duration of the device being plugged in. The
+data should be retrieved once during driver load and cached for subsequent queries.
+
+Byte   Bit 7                           Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2      Bit 1  Bit 0
+0...N
+                                                                0x00
+                                        Table 67: getInfo Request Packet
+
+Byte   Bit 7                           Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2      Bit 1  Bit 0
+  0                                                        SLOT_COUNT
+  1
+                                                         ACTUATOR_MASK
+2...N
+                                                                0x00
+                                       Table 68: getInfo Response Packet
+Logitech Force Feedback Protocol V1.6                                                 Page 28 of 44
+
+Parameter           Value                Deﬁnition
+                    0...255
+SLOT_COUNT          0...255              Indicates the number of available force eﬀect slots on the device.
+ACTUATOR_MASK                            A bitmask that indicates which axes have force feedback control.
+                                       Table 69: getInfo Response Parameters
+
+The axes of a device are assigned according to their prominence for the user and are generally device-speciﬁc. For the
+Logitech G920 wheel in particular, there is just one axis (the main wheel axis), hence the ACTUATOR_MASK value being
+0x01.
+
+4.1.2 x8123.1 resetAll
+The resetAll method stops and destroys all force eﬀects on the device. This method also enables the default centering
+spring. There are no parameters for either the request or the response.
+
+4.1.3 x8123.2 downloadEffect
+The downloadEffect method is used to create and update force eﬀects on the device. The ﬁrst byte decides if the eﬀect
+is to be allocated fresh (EFFECT_ID == 0), or if an existing eﬀect should be modiﬁed (0 < EFFECT_ID <= SLOT_COUNT).
+The ﬁrst byte of the response contains either 0x00 if there was an error, or the EFFECT_ID.
+Each slot controls an eﬀect for a given axis. There are no restrictions on how many slots are assigned to any axis, as long
+as the total number does not exceed SLOT_COUNT.
+
+             Byte            Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1         Bit 0
+               0
+               1                             EFFECT_ID
+
+             2...N           START     AXIS             EFFECT_TYPE
+
+                                       Parameters, depending on type of eﬀect
+
+                             Table 70: downloadEﬀect Request Packet Header
+
+Parameter          Value     Deﬁnition
+EFFECT_ID    0...SLOT_COUNT
+                             0x00: Create new eﬀect and return allocated eﬀect id.
+START               0/1      0x01...SLOT_COUNT: indicates id of eﬀect slot to update.
+AXIS               0...7     1: immediately start this eﬀect, 0: only create/update the eﬀect, do not start playing.
+EFFECT_TYPE    see table 72  The index of the axis, for which this force is applicable.
+                             One of the eﬀect types listed below.
+
+                                     Table 71: downloadEﬀect Parameters
+Logitech Force Feedback Protocol V1.6                                                                   Page 29 of 44
+
+Depending on the Eﬀect Type, the header is followed by one of three diﬀerent parameter formats.
+
+                              Value Eﬀect Type Parameter Format
+
+                              0 CONSTANT      Constant Parameters
+
+                              1 SINE          Periodic Parameters
+                              2 SQUARE
+                              3 TRIANGLE
+                              4 SAWTOOTHUP
+                              5 SAWTOOTHDOWN
+
+                              6 SPRING        Condition Parameters
+                              7 DAMPER
+                              8 FRICTION1
+                              9 INERTIA1
+
+                              10 RAMP1        Ramp Parameters
+
+                      Table 72: Eﬀect Type and Associated Parameter Format
+
+              Byte            Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1                          Bit 0
+                 0
+                 1                            EFFECT_ID
+                 2
+                 3            START     AXIS                    0x00
+                 4
+                 5                            DURATION15...8
+                 6
+                 7                            DURATION7...0
+                 8
+                 9                         START_DELAY15...8
+                10
+                11                            START_DELAY7...0
+                12
+                13                            LEVEL15...8
+
+              14...N                          LEVEL7...0
+
+                                              ATTACK_LEVEL
+
+                                          ATTACK_DELAY15...8

+4.1.4 x8123.3 setEffectState

+The setEffectState is used to start, stop, pause, and resume force eﬀects. The request speciﬁes an EFFECT_ID. If
+the slot does not contain a force, the response returns 0x00 as EFFECT_ID. Otherwise, the current state is returned in
+EFFECT_STATE.
+

+EFFECT_ID

+                    0...3              The requested new state of this eﬀect:         the eﬀect
+EFFECT_STATE        0...3              0x00 Do not change state, just return current
+                                       0x01 Stop the eﬀect
+                                       0x02 Resume (if paused) or Start (if stopped)
+                                       0x03 Pause the eﬀect
+                                       Returns the current state of the eﬀect:

+4.1.6 x8123.5 getAperture and x8123.6 setAperture
+The getAperture and setAperture methods are used to change the operating range of the wheel. Their parameter is a
+16-bit value measuring the operating range in degrees. For the Logitech G920, the valid range is 180...900. Any values
+outside this range will be clipped.
+

+4.1.7 x8123.7 getGlobalGains and x8123.8 setGlobalGains
+The getGlobalGains and setGlobalGains methods take two 16-bit values. Both values are coeﬃcients that are applied
+to any force output; however, they vary slightly. The ﬁrst value (GAIN) is a linear factor. The second value (BOOST) is
+oﬀset by a factor of 1.0:
+force = evaluateAllSlots ();
+force = force ∗ ( gain / 65535);
+force = force ∗ (1.0 + boost / 65535);
+outputForce ( force );
+Typically, the GAIN value is from the application/game. The BOOST value is controlled by the user via some value-add
+software (for example, on Windows, this is controlled by Logitech Gaming Software).
+
+Byte       Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1            Bit 0
+0...N
+                                              0x00
+                 Table 90: getGlobalGains Request Packet
+Logitech Force Feedback Protocol V1.6                                             Page 35 of 44
+
+Byte Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0
+
+0                                             GAIN15...8
+
+1                                             GAIN7...0
+
+2                                             BOOST15...8
+
+3                                             BOOST7...0
+
+4...N                                                    0x00
+
+Table 91: getGlobalGains Response and setGlobalGains Request/Re-
+sponse Packet
+
+                                       Parameter Value Deﬁnition
+
+                                       GAIN   0...65535  The force gain.
+                                       BOOST  0...65535  The force boost.
+
+           Table 92: getGlobalGains/setGlobalGains Parameters

+5 HID++ RPM Indicator
+
+The Logitech G923 wheels include an extra HID++ Feature for controlling the RPM LEDs. To get access to the feature
+the wheels must be put in to PC/classic mode. See the Appendix for further information on switching the wheels in to
+PC/classic mode.
+A HID++ command has a 4-byte header. After the header are the payload bytes of the speciﬁc method. The following
+description only lists the payload content for the requests and the responses.
+For more information on the HID++ header and the HID++ speciﬁcation see HID++ Header.
+
+5.1 x807a RPM Indicator
+
+The HID++ Feature x807a has the following methods and notiﬁcations:
+
+                                                           Index Name
+                                                              0 getInfo
+                                                              1 getModeInfo
+                                                              2 getMode
+                                                              3 setMode
+                                                              4 getLedColor
+                                                              5 setLedColor
+                                                              6 getRpm
+                                                              7 setRpm
+                                                              0 rpmModeChanged
+
+                                                Table 94: x807a Methods and Notiﬁcations
+
+5.1.1 x807a LED Modes
+The LED feature for a device supports one or more modes. The modes that the device supports can be retrieved with the
+x807a.1 getModeInfo command. The table below details the LED mode enumeration values.
+
+Mode         Value  Type               Description
+Direct         0    Direct
+InOut          1    Pattern            Allows leds to be updated in an arbitrary manner via setLedColor.
+OutIn          2    Pattern            LEDs are updated via setRpm. LEDs light up from the inside out.
+LeftToRight    3    Pattern            LEDs are updated via setRpm. LEDs light up from the outside in.
+RightToLeft    4    Pattern            LEDs are updated via setRpm. LEDs light up from left to right.
+                                       LEDs are updated via setRpm. LEDs light up form right to left.
+
+                                                Table 95: modes
+Logitech Force Feedback Protocol V1.6                                                   Page 37 of 44
+
+5.1.2 x807a LED Types
+Diﬀerent devices support diﬀerent types of LEDs. The type of LED that the device supports can be retrieved with the
+x807a.0 getInfo command. The table below details the LED Types enumeration values.
+
+LED Type  Value  Description
+Unknown     0
+RGB888      1    Unknown LED type
+Mono8       2    RGB LEDs: Stored in 3 bytes of data. Full 24bit color pallet.
+Mono1       3    Mono LED with brightness: Stored in 1 byte of data.
+                 Mono LED: Stored in 1 bit of data. Mono LED that is either on or oﬀ.
+
+                                  Table 96: LED types
+
