+A Appendix
+
+A.1 HID++ Header
+
+A HID++ command has a 4-byte header, which includes the HID REPORT_ID, the device index (only used for HID++ hub
+devices; specify 0xff for the wheels), the index of the feature, the index of the method, and a session identiﬁer. After
+the header are the payload bytes of the speciﬁc method. The following description only lists the payload content for the
+requests and the responses.
+
+Follow this link for further information on the HID++ Speciﬁcation.
+
+               Byte         Bit 7 Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0
+                 0          Report id (0x10 short (7 bytes) 0x11 long (20 bytes))
+                 1
+                 2                                   Device index (0xff)
+                 3                              Feature index (0x00 - 0xff)
+                              Software id (0x0 - 0xf) Method index (0x0 - 0xf)
+               4...N
+                                                    Payload data from here
+                                       Table 110: HID++ 4-byte header.
+
+Parameter         Value     Deﬁnition
+Report id      0x10 - 0x12  0x10 short (7 bytes) 0x11 long (20 bytes) 0x12 verylong (60 bytes).
+Device index                Only used for HID++ hub devices. specify 0xﬀ.
+Feature index       0xﬀ     The index of the feature. This has to be obtained from the device.
+               0x00 - 0xﬀ   For more details see the HID++ speciﬁcation.
+Software id                 Can be any valid, number and is used to match up the psend packet with the response packet.
+Method index    0x0 - 0xf   The index of the feature function. See the methods and notiﬁcations table for the feature.
+                0x0 - 0xf
+
+A.2 Classic Compatibility Mode
+
+In order to support older console games, most Logitech Force Feedback steering wheels connect initially in a compatibility
+mode. Typically, this mode equals a Logitech Driving Force, but in some circumstances it may also look like a G25 or
+Driving Force Pro.
+
+The table below lists the checks required to identify a particular wheel, when it is in compatibility mode. The checks
+should be done in the order listed, as they become progressively less speciﬁc.
+
+Wheel                       Native PID            Identiﬁcation Criteria when in compatibility mode
+Logitech Driving Force GT     0xC29A
+Logitech G27 Racing Wheel     0xC29B                  ((0xC294 == PID) || (0xC298 == PID))
+Logitech G25 Racing Wheel     0xC299                                            &&
+Logitech Driving Force Pro    0xC298
+                                                         (0x1300 == (bcdDevice & 0xff00))
+
+                                        ((0xC294 == PID) || (0xC298 == PID) || (0xC299 == PID))
+                                                                                &&
+
+                                                         (0x1230 == (bcdDevice & 0xfff0))
+
+                                                      ((0xC294 == PID) || (0xC298 == PID))
+                                                                                &&
+
+                                                         (0x1200 == (bcdDevice & 0xff00))
+
+                                                                      (0xC294 == PID)
+                                                                                &&
+
+                                                         (0x1000 == (bcdDevice & 0xf000))
+Logitech Force Feedback Protocol V1.6                                            Page 43 of 44
+
+Wheel         Native PID               Identiﬁcation Criteria when in compatibility mode
+
+                                       Table 112: Device Identiﬁcation Criteria
+
+Once a wheel has been identiﬁed, it can be switched to its native mode using the appropriate switch command. The
+following table lists the supported commands for each wheel. The Logitech Driving Force GT (PID_C29A) and Log-
+itech G27 Racing Wheel (PID_C29B) also support the RevertIdentity command, so that subsequent USB resets will not
+have to change mode again – the wheel will come up in native mode right-away.
+
+       Wheel                           Switch command(s) Byte Sequence
+
+       Logitech Driving Force GT       EXT_CMD 0x0a  0xf8 0x0a 0x00 - optional
+                                       EXT_CMD 0x09  0xf8 0x09 0x03 0x01
+
+       Logitech G27 Racing Wheel       EXT_CMD 0x0a  0xf8 0x0a 0x00 - optional
+                                       EXT_CMD 0x09  0xf8 0x09 0x04 0x01
+
+       Logitech G25 Racing Wheel EXT_CMD 0x10 0xf8 0x10
+
+       Logitech Driving Force Pro      EXT_CMD 0x01 0xf8 0x01
+
+                                       Table 113: Mode Switch Command
+Logitech Force Feedback Protocol V1.6                                                    Page 44 of 44
+
+A.3 Xbox Mode
+
+The Logitech G920 and G923 Xbox wheels start out by default in Xbox Force Feedback Mode. To switch to HID++
+Mode, issue the following byte sequence as an output report to the USB endpoint:
+
+                             Wheel          Byte Sequence
+
+                             Logitech G920  0x0f 0x00 0x01 0x01 0x42
+
+                             Logitech G923 Xbox 0x0f 0x00 0x01 0x01 0x42
+                               Table 114: Xbox wheel Mode Switch Command
+
+A.4 Playstation Mode
+
+The Logitech G929 and G923 Playstation wheels start out by default in Playstation Force Feedback Mode. To switch to
+Classic Mode, issue the following byte sequence as an output report to the USB endpoint:
+
+Wheel                        Byte Sequence
+Logitech
+          G29 Playstation 3  Send this report ﬁrst 0x21, 0x09, 0x00, 0x02, 0x00,  0x00,  0x10,  0x00
+Logitech                     Send this report second 0xf8, 0x09, 0x05, 0x01
+Logitech
+          G29 Playstation 4 0x30, 0xf8, 0x09, 0x05, 0x01
+
+          G923 Playstation 0x30, 0xf8, 0x09, 0x05, 0x01
+                               Table 115: Playstation wheel Mode Switch Command
+
